# run9 英語レビュー: ワールド29・30（レッスン 841〜900）

- 日付: 2026-11-27
- やりかた: docs/run9/BRIEF.md の「Review method」。lint → 3人の レビュアー（A=アメリカの 小学校・中学校の 先生 / B=こどもの ESL・言語習得の 専門家 / C=台本を 読む 声優）を べつべつに 書いて から 決める。2人 いじょうが 指摘 or 客観的な まちがい（objective）だけ なおす。10 レッスンずつ 6 グループで レビュー
- lint（`npm run english:lint -- --from 841 --to 900`）: **まえ error 0 / warn 15 / info 9 → あと error 0 / warn 14 / info 5**（まえの warn: 「…」14・stiff「Please enjoy」1。あとの のこりは ぜんぶ わざと: タイトル「I Think So Because…」「I See Your Point, But…」「So You Mean…」「In 2050…」と その スライド〈テストで きまって いる〉、会話の「…」〈ためらい・ま〉。info は 会話の「What is」2 と「Let's」4回の レッスン 3〈L854「Let's vote」・L855 など 学習項目〉）
- なおした 欄: **436**（パッチ 453 件。機械的な なおし 2・ブロックの きめごと 78・AI の さいしょの ひとこと 60・レビュアー 268・あとの そろえ 45）。なおした レッスン: **60 / 60**
- 60 レッスン ぜんぶに `"reviewed": "run9"` を つけた
- パッチは ぜんぶ「before が いまの 値と おなじ ときだけ あてる」で、HEAD から じゅんばんに あてなおした（はずれ 0）。60 レッスン ぜんぶで スライドの 検証（unknownSlideItems）・台本の 長さ（180〜240 秒）・英語の わりあいを たしかめた（ALL OK）。かいわ あいての チェック（.run9-w2930/partner.ts）も ALL OK

## 共通の きめごと（ブロック全体）
1. 話しことばの「Yes, exactly. Or, Not quite.」→「Or not quite.」（L857）
2. イギリスふうの ほめことば: 「Brilliant!」→「Great job! / Fantastic!」（L844・L845・L858・L864・L874・L894）、「Lovely work!」→「Great work!」（L846・L859）、「Lovely!」→「Great! / Nice!」（L846・L862）、「What a lovely tour!」→「What a great tour!」（L869）
3. エマ先生 じぶんの 語りの「Ms. Emma says / asks / explains / has a plan」→「I say / I ask / I explain / I have a plan」、日本語「エマ先生「」→「わたし「」（L847・L855・L862・L868・L882・L886・L887）
4. エマ先生の あいさつの「Good morning / Good evening」→「Hi / Hello」・日本語「やあ / こんにちは」（L843・L857・L859・L860・L873・L895）
5. ノバの カタカナを「ノバ」に そろえた（L891〜L900 は「ノヴァ」だった。30 か所）
6. **AI の さいしょの ひとこと**: アプリは あいてを こどもの キャラ（ハル・リコ・キコ・ビリモ）から えらび、その かおと なまえで ひとことを 出す。なのに 60 レッスン ぜんぶが「I'm Captain Pell.」「I'm Nova.」「Ahoy!」「Beep!」など べつの 人として 話して いた。goalJa が やくを きめて いるので やくは のこし、「Let's pretend I'm Captain Pell / Gus / Gale / Pip / Nova / Ms. Lin / Tess / a robot teacher from the future.」で ごっこあそびだと わかる ように した（W25-28 と おなじ）。L877 は goalJa の とおり ノバに した。2つめの Let's は「Time for / It's time for / We can」に、lint の info（that is・I am・Please show me）も ついでに なおした
7. あとの そろえ（グループの 報告から。20-after.json）:
   - 「Your turn! Maybe, ～」→「Your turn! You could say, ～」（W30 の 38 欄。W29 は グループが おなじ かたちに。Maybe, だと こどもが じぶんの 文に じしんが ない ように きこえる）
   - L882 energetic の 声の 音節「en-er-GET-ic」→「en-er-JET-ic」（g は やわらかい 音。L898 と おなじ。かく 音節の 表 w30Phonics.ts と tipJa は つづりの ままで のこした）
   - L871「many years later」→「many years from now」（これからの ことなので）
   - L875 看板の「Please enjoy both kinds of trees!」→「Enjoy both kinds of trees!」（lint stiff-phrase）
8. グループで ブロック全体に あてた もの: ワールド29 の ことばの せつめいに のこって いた 関係代名詞（「Someone who helps」「An animal that lives」「A toy that spins」など。テストの 正規表現は segments を みない）と、W30 で ならう まえの L871〜L875 の「A machine that ～」「The person who drives」を ふつうの 文に。-ic の 強勢を「e, LEC, tric」の コンマ区切りから「e-LEC-tric」に（L881〜L900）

## 学習項目 ID が かわった もの
- なし（表現・ことば・TPR・フォニックスの ことば・タイトルは かえて いない。テスト・src の 変更も なし）

## のこした こと（1人だけの 指摘・テストで きまって いる・あとで 決める）
- タイトルと スライドの「…」、会話の「…」
- 「before ic」「Words that end in ic」の たんどくの ic（TTS が「アイ・シー」と よむか きいて たしかめたい）、強勢の「SO」「DO」（L851〜L855。文字で よまれないか きいて たしかめたい）
- 弱形の つづりの ゆれ（teacherScript の「fur」「un」と segments の「fer」「n」。1グループだけ）
- L863 core「I read it on a note at the harbor.」の read（TTS が「リード」と よむ かも しれないが 学習項目）
- L867 ことば「holiday」（「やすみの 日」の いみで アメリカでも つかう。summer vacation は べつに ある）、L900「World 30 Invention Expo」、「Say thank you with a bow」（W19 と おなじ TPR）、「Ahoy!」「shipshape」「Beep!」

## レビュアーの メモ（グループごと・英語）

### Run 9 review notes: L841-850 (world 29, Stage 4)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide), plus testPoints. I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (US praise, I say / I ask, Hi / Hello openings, "Let's pretend I'm" starters). There are 56 patches in all 10 lessons; no lesson is left without a change. There are no learning-item ID changes and no changes to school.expressions[].en, vocab[].en, tpr[].en or phonics words, so no test or src line is affected. The grammar order is clean: "For example" first appears in L843, "One / Another reason is" in L844, "agree with" in L846, "I see your point" in L847, "not sure about that" in L848, "partly agree" in L849 and "I disagree" in L850. Themes: (1) **"Maybe, ..." before the model sentence** in every "Your turn!" segment (L842-850 seg17): "Maybe, I think rabbits are great pets..." sounds like the child is hedging the opinion and TTS reads it that way; changed to "You could say, ..." (L848: "You could ask, ... Then say, ..."). L843's JA had たとえば twice in a row, and the L845 model asked for an example but gave none (added "For example, I sing with my family."). (2) **Relative clauses in W29 segments** that the test regex does not see (it checks only teacherScript, expressions, TPR, conversation and starter): "A person who watches" (L841, L845), "An animal that lives" (L842), "A person who looks inside" (L842), "A real thing that shows" (L843), "A book that says" (L846), "Two things that say" (L849). Each is now a plain sentence, mostly the wording the teacherScript already uses. (3) **Word-root meanings**: L841 seg10 defined suspect as "Look secretly" (the teacherScript already has the right meaning); L843 "The doctor does an inspection" (doctors do checkups); L847 "The final answer, like a judge says". (4) **Small story and wording fixes**: L846 Riko asked "Do you agree with me?" before giving her opinion; L847 Gus's line had no "He says,"; L847 "Gus, please say a reason."; L847 wrap-up "but, and say,"; L848 goal "Speak up when you can't decide"; L850 game card "Stand on the left for agree". Every new sentence has 15 words or fewer. The check script passes with ALL OK. All lessons are 213-229 s long with 100% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L841 The Lighthouse of Ideas
- A: The arrival, Anchor at the door, Captain Pell the pelican and the old sign are warm and natural. seg10 "Suspect. Look secretly." is not what suspect means; a child would learn the wrong word. Ms. Emma's conversation line "I think we can help because we're good at listening." is fine before L842 (no test rule on I think).
- B: The spect = look tip and the four words are right; the teacherScript gives suspect correctly ("You think someone did something wrong"), so seg10 contradicts it. seg10 "A person who watches a game" is a relative clause in W29 (segments are not in the test regex, but the brief prefers a plain sentence).
- C: Reads well. seg8 "Why is it so weak? asks for a reason." makes a question run into "asks" (single flag, kept). "spect" alone on the slide is fine; the say lines always use it inside "Spect means look".
- Decision: seg10 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Spectator. A person watching a game. Suspect. You think someone did something wrong. Hmm, something's strange! ..." and subtitleJa suspect。だれかが わるい ことを したと おもう こと。(A+B). tipJa's こっそり 見る etymology is kept (it ends in あやしいと おもう, which is right).

#### L842 I Think So Because…
- A: The galley stove, the Pet Talk board and Birimo's cake pet are fun. seg17 "Maybe, I think rabbits are great pets..." is not how a teacher offers a model; "You could say, ..." is.
- B: I think ~ because ~ is clearly built (opinion, then because + reason). The loyal（ちゅうじつ） hint is the allowed one. seg3 "An animal that lives with you" and seg9 "A person who looks inside" are W29 relative clauses. The quiz distractor "which" is fine.
- C: "Maybe, I think" reads as hedging. "Bye-bye!" is fine for kids.
- Decision: seg3 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Pet. A pet lives with you at home, like a dog, a cat or a rabbit. Pet!" and seg9 -> "Inspector. An inspector looks inside and checks things." (objective, W29 rule). seg17 "Maybe," -> "You could say," (A+B+C).

#### L843 For Example
- A: Captain Pell's notebook ("An example is like a picture") is a lovely reading. seg9 "The doctor does an inspection." is odd in US English (a doctor gives a checkup; buildings and lighthouses get inspections). seg17 "Maybe, I think birds are cool."
- B: For example appears first here (tested). seg2 "A real thing that shows what you mean" is a W29 relative clause. seg17 JA reads たとえば「…。たとえば、…」 with たとえば twice.
- C: Reads well apart from the "Maybe," opener.
- Decision: seg2 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Example. It shows what you mean." with JA いいたい ことを わかりやすく しめして くれるよ (objective). seg9 -> "The lighthouse gets an inspection every year." (the teacherScript's example) with JA とうだいは まいとし けんさを うけるよ (A+B). seg17 "Maybe," -> "You could say," and JA たとえば「 -> こんなふうに いえるよ。「 (A+B+C / objective JA).

#### L844 Reasons Make It Strong
- A: The two posts (cats are quiet, you don't need to walk them; dogs love to play) are natural. "Put on your spectacles." is old-fashioned but it is the phonics word and the tipJa says so (kept). seg20 "give them with one reason and another reason" is a bit clunky (single flag, kept).
- B: One reason is that / Another reason is that is introduced here (tested) and the two-fingers gesture is clear. "A good aspect of cats is their quiet voice" is fine.
- C: seg17 "Maybe, One reason is that..." has the hedging opener.
- Decision: seg17 (say, subtitleEn) "Maybe," -> "You could say," (A+B+C).

#### L845 The Light Gets Brighter
- A: The lamp room, the first lens and Captain Pell's log are good; "Tonight" and "Day Five" are inside the log (story-internal, kept). seg17 asks for "Your opinion, a reason and an example" but the model gives no example.
- B: Same flag: the model does not show the full three-step pattern taught today. seg10 "A person who watches" is a W29 relative clause; the teacherScript already says "A person watching."
- C: Reads well; "Maybe," opener again.
- Decision: seg10 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Spectator. A person watching." (objective). seg17 (say, subtitleEn) -> "You could say, I think singing is fun. One reason is that it makes me happy. For example, I sing with my family." with JA to match (A+B+C).

#### L846 I Agree with You
- A: The library, Book Talk Time poster and "Snacks are OK, but please keep the books clean!" are natural. In the teacherScript Riko asks "Do you agree with me?" before saying what she thinks ("Books are better at night."); the conversation has the right order.
- B: Same flag (you cannot agree with an opinion you have not heard). agree with appears first here (tested). The dict = say tip is right (pre = before, contra = against). seg10 "A book that says what words mean" is a W29 relative clause.
- C: "Maybe, I agree with you." opener.
- Decision: teacherScript -> "Riko says, I think books are better at night. Do you agree with me?" (A+B). seg10 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Dictionary. It tells you what words mean." (objective). seg17 "Maybe," -> "You could say," (A+B+C).

#### L847 I See Your Point, But…
- A: Gus and Gale arguing and calming down is a good story; Gus and Gale are in the teacherScript (tested). Haru's "Gus, please say a reason." is textbook English; a child says "Can you give us a reason?". seg10 "The final answer, like a judge says." is not grammatical. seg20 "with I see your point, but, and say," is hard to follow.
- B: Same flags. "Gus tries first. I think the mountains are more fun..." has no speaker tag, so Gus's words read as the narrator's. The verdict (ver = true) and dictation explanations are right. "My prediction is rain tomorrow" is casual but fine (single flag, kept).
- C: The teacherScript line without "He says," and "but, and say," both trip the reader.
- Decision: teacherScript "Gus tries first. He says, I think..." (A+C). conversation line 1, seg6 (say, subtitleEn, slide item) -> "Gus, can you give us a reason?" with JA ガス、りゆうを おしえて くれる？ (A+B). seg10 -> "Verdict. A judge's final answer." with JA さいばんかんの さいごの こたえ (A+B). seg17 "Maybe," -> "You could say," (A+B+C). seg20 -> "...with I see your point, but I think. You can also say, Let's listen to each other." (A+C).

#### L848 I'm Not Sure About That
- A: The Big Question card (Little Crab, Old Turtle, Young Rabbit and the carrots) is charming. seg1 "Speak up when you can't decide." means "talk louder / voice your view", not "what to say when undecided". "I'm not sure about that" fits here because it answers a claim ("the mountains are more fun, right?").
- B: Same seg1 flag; the JA already says きめられない ときの いいかた. Conversation line 4 has Birimo ask and answer "Which is better, cake or ice cream? Hmm, it's hard to decide." himself; it is in character (the snack lover is torn) and the teacherScript has Haru answer (single flag, kept). predictable / unpredictable / dictate are right.
- C: seg17 "Maybe, Which is better, summer or winter? Hmm, ..." runs a question and its answer together after "Maybe,".
- Decision: seg1 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Learn what to say when you can't decide." (A+B). seg17 -> "You could ask, Which is better, summer or winter? Then say, Hmm, it's hard to decide." with JA と きいて、…と こたえよう (A+B+C).

#### L849 Partly Agree
- A: The calm summer talk and the Season Talk notes are natural ("my ice cream melts too fast!"). "Yesterday, I predicted rain, and it rained!" is an example sentence, not schedule pacing (kept).
- B: Partly = a part, not all; good part, then but, then the other part is clear. The y -> i + es rule for dictionaries is right, and -ed after t sounds like id (tipJa ティッド, seg10 "tid") is right. seg9 "Two things that say opposite things" is a W29 relative clause with "things" twice; the teacherScript has "Two ideas say opposite things."
- C: seg10 "The end sounds like tid." is readable by TTS (kept). "Y changes to I, plus E S" reads fine.
- Decision: seg9 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Contradiction. Two ideas say opposite things." (objective). seg17 "Maybe," -> "You could say," (A+B+C).

#### L850 Agree or Disagree Game
- A: The beach game, the second lens and "there is no wrong side" are lovely. The game card's "Stand on the left for agree. Stand on the right for disagree." is not grammatical.
- B: Same flag; the TPR already says "Stand on the left if you agree." I disagree because appears first here (tested). The review of dict and spect is correct. The starter ("Winter is the best season. Do you agree?") works for the partner child Riko.
- C: "Say, I agree because, or, I disagree because, and give a reason." is comma-heavy but reads as quoted frames (kept). "Maybe, I disagree..." opener.
- Decision: teacherScript card -> "Stand on the left if you agree. Stand on the right if you disagree." (A+B). seg17 "Maybe," -> "You could say," (A+B+C).

#### Block-wide notes
- **"Maybe, <model sentence>" in seg17 "Your turn!"**: fixed in L842-850. Please grep W29-30 for `Maybe, ` in say / subtitleEn (pattern `Your turn!.*Maybe, [A-Z]`) and use "You could say, ..." (or "You could ask, ... Then say, ..." when the model is a question and answer). Check the JA for a doubled たとえば when the model itself has For example.
- **Relative clauses in W29 segments**: the W29 test regex covers only teacherScript, expressions, TPR, conversation and starter. Segment definitions like "A person who ~", "An animal that ~", "A book that ~", "Two things that ~" slip through. Grep L851-870 segments for `\b(who|that|which) [a-z]+s\b` and use "A person ~ing" or "It ~s".
- **Root-word definitions**: compare each segment's definition with the teacherScript's (L841 suspect had "Look secretly" in seg10 but the right meaning in the script).
- Kept on purpose: L841 seg8 "Why is it so weak? asks for a reason."; L844 "Put on your spectacles." (phonics word) and the seg20 wording; L845 log "Tonight"; L847 "My prediction is rain tomorrow."; L848 Birimo asking and answering himself; L849 "Yesterday, I predicted rain" (example) and "tid"; L850 comma-heavy frames on the game card; all "…" in titles and slides, the L842 JA hint, and "Bye-bye!".

### Run 9 review notes: L851-860 (world 29, Stage 4)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide), plus testPoints. I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (L857 "Or not quite.", US praise, I say / I ask, Hi / Hello openings, "Let's pretend I'm Captain Pell." starters). There are 46 patches in 9 lessons; L851 has no change. There are no learning-item ID changes and no changes to school.expressions[].en, vocab[].en, tpr[].en or phonics words. The grammar order is right: "let's hear from" first appears in L852, "go ahead" in L853, "let's vote" in L854, "what do you mean" in L856, "so you mean" in L857, "give me an example" in L858, "let me think" in L859; no fact/opinion wording appears before L861. Themes: (1) **relative clauses in W29 word segments**: the test regex only checks teacherScript, expressions, TPR, conversation and starters, so vocab segments still had "the person who leads" (L852 chair), "things that make" (L853 decoration), "A person who watches / cooks" (L855 quiz), "A person who works" (L856 sailor), "One thing that shows" and "Someone who helps" (L858), and "A person who takes care of" (L860 keeper). I changed all of them to plain sentences ("A helper helps.", "Sailors work on ships."). (2) **Story and speaker slips**: L859 lanterns "floating on the sea" (they hang on the boats); L860 seg7 "Then Anchor loves bones and cake" with no speaker; L857 seg6 "I say, Captain Pell says," stacked two speech frames. (3) **Small wording**: L852 "Use two friends", L853 "get in", L854 "Ask, Raise your hand", L855 "goes out to learn somewhere fun", L858 speed cue "Hand out!", L859 "And and is tiny", L860 "predict means say before". (4) **Schedule and JA**: L855 "Back home, we will have a real class meeting soon" assumed the child's schedule, and the JA おうちに かえったら put the class meeting at home. (5) **Heteronym**: L856 "Bow means the front of a ship" removed (bow / bow / bow; only use in W29). Every new sentence has 12 words or fewer. The check script passes with ALL OK. All changed lessons are 221-227 s long with 100% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L851 What Do You Think?
- A: The round meeting room, the festival notice and the three ideas (lanterns, kite, giant cake) are natural. Kiko's "Birimo, that's your idea, not Captain Pell's!" is a fun use of the playing frame. "a big kite is fun too" is fine.
- B: Name at the end ("What do you think, Kiko?") and "How about you?" to pass the turn are clear and well explained. The stress tip (really, totally, so, love) is correct. The quiz answer slide shows the Kiko line for a Riko question, but it is a valid item (kept).
- C: The capital stress words read well, except a small risk that "SO" (seg10, also L854) could be read as letters by some voices (single flag, kept; see block notes).
- Decision: no change.

#### L852 Let's Hear from Everyone
- A: The shouting seagulls and the speaking shell are a nice classroom routine; Captain Pell's chair card is natural. seg17 "Use two friends. You can make up names." is not what a teacher says (you use their names).
- B: seg2 "The chair is the person who leads the meeting." is a relative clause in W29 (the regex misses "leads"); the teacherScript already says "The chair leads the meeting." seg17 has the same flag as A; the JA says ともだち ふたりの なまえで.
- C: "Not this kind! Ha ha." reads fine. "Shell. A pretty white thing from the beach." is fine (the story shell is white).
- Decision: seg2 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Here, a chair is a person. The chair leads the meeting." (objective, W29 rule). seg17 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Use two friends' names. You can make them up, too." (A+B).

#### L853 Taking Turns
- A: The Round Room Rules poster is natural and kind (shy friends can write a card). seg0 "How can she get in?" sounds like getting into a room.
- B: seg0 same flag; seg1 already says "Join in politely" and the JA says はなしに 入ろう. seg2 "Pretty things that make a place look happy" is a relative clause in W29. The I DO agree tip is explained well.
- C: "Listen to DO." and "I DO agree" may make some voices hesitate on a two-letter capital word (single flag, kept).
- Decision: seg0 (say, subtitleEn) "get in" -> "join in" (A+B). seg2 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Decoration. Something pretty for a party or a festival. Decoration!" with JA パーティーや おまつりを かざる きれいな もの (objective, W29 rule).

#### L854 Let's Decide
- A: The picnic vote and Gus's kind "Maybe the beach next time" are warm. The vote board numbers agree with every line (6 + 3 + 1 = 10). seg1 "Ask, Raise your hand if you like the beach." is not a question.
- B: seg1 also drops a lead-in before "Most people chose the park. It's decided!" so the child does not know when to say it (the JA has けっかは). "Your idea was not chosen? That's OK." is a good social point. Captain Pell raising his wing is right (he is a pelican, L841).
- C: "You are SO right" (phonics word, a table key) is fine aloud.
- Decision: seg1 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Say, Let's vote. Then say, Raise your hand if you like the beach. Give the result. Most people chose the park. It's decided!" with JA to match (A+B).

#### L855 Class Meeting Role-Play
- A: The class meeting role-play and Kiko's notes are natural (5 + 3 + 2 = 10 votes; Birimo claps for the aquarium). The teacherScript "Back home, we will have a real class meeting soon." and seg0 "Back home, you will have real class meetings." assume the child's school schedule. seg2 "The whole class goes out to learn somewhere fun" is awkward.
- B: The JA おうちに かえったら says the class meeting is at home (EN/JA mismatch). seg18 quiz "A person who watches a game. B, A person who cooks dinner." is two relative clauses in W29. The dict / spect review is correct.
- C: seg2 "learn somewhere fun" is a stumble. Bare "Dict" / "Spect" are pronounceable and are the block pattern (kept).
- Decision: teacherScript -> "Today, I have a plan. At school, you may have class meetings, too. Let's practice here!" (A+B). seg0 (say, subtitleEn) -> "At school, you may have class meetings, too." (A+B), JA 学校でも クラス かいぎが ある かもしれないね (objective JA). seg2 (say, subtitleEn) -> "The whole class goes somewhere fun to learn." (A+C). seg18 (say, subtitleEn) -> "What does a spectator do? A, Watches a game. B, Cooks dinner." with JA to match (objective, W29 rule).

#### L856 What Do You Mean?
- A: Cool (a little cold / great) and the galley are a great pair; "When you don't know a word, just ask. It isn't rude." is a good message. The sailor word list is fun.
- B: "Bow means the front of a ship" is a homograph the child already knows as a ribbon or a greeting; it adds a hard word with no use later. seg5 "Sailor. A person who works on a ship." is a relative clause in W29. The weak can tip is correct.
- C: TTS will often read "Bow" as /boʊ/. "kun" in seg9/seg10 is the block respelling for the weak form (like "tuh", "fer"); kept.
- Decision: teacherScript "Bow means the front of a ship, and stern means the back." -> "Stern means the back of a ship." (B+C; the quote is still over 200 characters). seg5 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Sailor. Sailors work on ships. Ahoy! Sailor!" (objective, W29 rule).

#### L857 So You Mean…
- A: The fog plan and Captain Pell's festival day timetable are clear and consistent (breakfast at seven, finish before lunch, fog goes away in the evening). seg6 "I say, Captain Pell says, Thick fog ..." stacks two speech frames (left over from the block I say fix).
- B: "So you mean" is well explained (say it in your own words to check). In seg6 "Yes, exactly." has no speaker; in the conversation Ms. Emma says it. "Thick fog comes after lunch tomorrow" uses the present for a forecast; acceptable (single flag, kept).
- C: seg6 "I say, Captain Pell says," is hard to read aloud. "Not TOO, but tuh" reads well.
- Decision: seg6 (say, subtitleEn) -> "I share Captain Pell's news. Thick fog comes after lunch tomorrow. Haru thinks. So you mean we should start early? I say, Yes, exactly. Haru says, Got it!" with JA わたしが ペルせんちょうの おしらせを つたえるよ / わたし「うん、その とおり。」 (A+C).

#### L858 Could You Give an Example?
- A: Festival morning and the two helper posts are natural; "Big or small, fast or slow, every helper is special!" is nice. The speed cue "Hand out!" means give things out.
- B: seg2 "One thing that shows the idea" and seg3 "Someone who helps" are relative clauses in W29 ("someone who helps" is exactly the test regex pattern, only outside the checked fields). seg13 same flag as A.
- C: "Not FOUR, but fer" is a good contrast. The teacherScript says "It sounds like fur." while the segments say "fer"; both read the same (kept, see block notes).
- Decision: seg2 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Example. It shows your idea." (objective, W29 rule). seg3 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Helper. A helper helps." (objective, W29 rule). seg13 (say, subtitleEn) "Hand out!" -> "Hold out your hand!" (A+B).

#### L859 Good Question!
- A: The Question Box on festival night is lovely and natural ("There are no wrong answers in this box."). seg0 "lanterns are floating on the sea" contradicts the story: the lanterns hang on the boats (L853, the L857 plan, this teacherScript "Lanterns glow on the little boats").
- B: Taking time to think is well modeled. seg20 "And and is tiny now." is confusing for a learner.
- C: "And and" is a stumble aloud. "Come-n-see" reads naturally.
- Decision: seg0 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Look, lanterns are glowing on the boats." with JA ふねの ちょうちんが ひかって いるよ (objective, story). seg20 (say, subtitleEn) -> "And the word and is tiny now." (A+C).

#### L860 Interview with Follow-Ups
- A: The interview card and the foggy-night story are natural and consistent (Anchor waits at the door every day, as in L843). seg7 "Because families wait for them. ... Then Anchor loves bones and cake." has no speaker, so it reads as narration. seg20 "predict means say before" is not grammatical.
- B: seg4 "A person who takes care of a place" is a relative clause in W29. seg7 and seg20 same flags as A. "How long has he been a keeper? For forty years." is fine at Stage 4. The conversation has 8 lines (allowed).
- C: seg7 is confusing aloud without "Birimo says".
- Decision: seg4 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Keeper. A keeper takes care of a place." (objective, W29 rule). seg7 (say, subtitleEn, subtitleJa) -> "Birimo says, Because families wait for them. A lighthouse is a friend for boats. Then he says, Anchor loves bones and cake." (A+B). seg20 (say, subtitleEn, subtitleJa) -> "And you know predict. Pre means before, and dict means say!" (A+B).

#### Block-wide notes
- **Relative clauses in W29 vocab and quiz segments**: the test regex does not check teacher.segments, and its noun list misses "person who leads/works/takes/watches", "things that", "one thing that". Please grep W29 segments for `\b(person|people|someone|something|things?|animals?|place) (who|that|which) ` and use plain sentences ("A helper helps."). Contact clauses without who/that ("Something you ask", "Something new you think of") were kept.
- **Stacked speech frames from the I say fix**: L857 had "I say, Captain Pell says, ...". Grep the block for `I say, [A-Z][a-z]+ (says|asks)`.
- **Weak-form respellings**: the block uses "kun" (can), "tuh" (to), "fer" (for), "n" (and) inside example sentences in say and subtitleEn. They read fine; but the teacherScripts say "kun", "tuh", "fur" (L858) and "un" (L859) while the segments say "fer" and "n". If you want one form per word, pick one; I did not change them.
- **Two-letter capital stress words**: "SO" (L851, L854) and "DO" (L853, L855) are the only all-caps words in my group that a voice might spell out; longer ones (REALLY, ABSOLUTELY) are safe. Kept; check with the real TTS voice.
- **Bare "Dict" / "Spect"** (55 hits in world-29) are the block pattern and pronounceable; kept.
- Kept on purpose: all "…" in titles and conversation lines; "Ahoy!", "Aye, aye!", "shipshape", "galley"; "Thick fog comes after lunch tomorrow" (forecast present); "You are so right" and other phonics words (table keys); "I think listening is the most important" (expression); goalJa "AI の ペル船長" (fits the "Let's pretend I'm Captain Pell." starters); L852 white shell; L855 "A meeting is easy."; L859 "A slow, kind answer is a great answer."

### Run 9 review notes: L861-870 (world 29, Stage 4)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide), plus testPoints. I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (Or not quite, US praise, I say / I ask, Hi / Hello openings, ノバ, "Let's pretend I'm" starters). There are 48 patches in 9 lessons; L869 has no change. There are no learning-item ID changes and no changes to school.expressions[].en, vocab[].en, tpr[].en or phonics words. Every partner check still holds (wrap-up "talk with X" matches the app's partner), and no starter names a child. Themes: (1) **The -ible rule was stated as a law**: "poss / vis / terr / horr / incred / ed / aud / respons are not words by themselves, so they take ible" (L861-864, teacherScript, tipJa, L861 seg10). It is only a tendency (flexible, sensible, capable, probable), and src/lib/curriculum/w29Phonics.ts already says ことが おおい; I added "usually" / ことが おおい. (2) **Relative clauses in W29** that the test regex misses: "where boats stop" (L863), "pictures that want", "a toy that spins" (L864), "a group that works", "glass that makes" (L865), "an animal that lives" (L867); all turned into plain sentences. Contact clauses ("Pages of news you can read", "Something fun you play with", "Schoolwork you do at home") are kept; they are natural and not the taught pattern. (3) **Read-aloud problems**: "Say STRONGLY strongly!" (L866), "Make love big." (L867), the invented "stop n think" (L868), "Someone read lake as cake" (L863, heteronym and hard for kids). (4) **Story and logic**: Pip's L870 story gave Kiko's line to Birimo; the L863 starter said Pip "heard" a rumor while the target asks "Where did you read that?". (5) **Small wording and JA**: "a small paper", "In a book, or ask a grown-up", "in the summer vacation", verdict = "final answer", "School work", それとも in a plain list, ぜんぜん おなじ, かしこい おかいものの みんな. Every new sentence has 10 words or fewer. The check script passes with ALL OK. All changed lessons are 219-228 s long with 100% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L861 Fact or Opinion?
- A: Pip's newsroom, the cat fact and opinion and the Page Two story are natural and fun ("Birimos are the cutest!"). seg8 "Can we check it? It's a fact. Is it a thought or a feeling? It's an opinion." sounds like a question and its answer, not a rule; a teacher would say "Then it's a fact."
- B: The definitions are right (a fact can be checked; an opinion is an idea or feeling, and others can feel differently; neither is called wrong). seg8 has the same flag; the JA says それなら. "Poss and vis are not words by themselves, so they take ible" (teacherScript, seg10, tipJa) states a false absolute rule (flexible, sensible take -ible from real words; capable, probable take -able without one).
- C: Reads well. "Cats sleep a lot is a fact" without quotes is house style and TTS reads it fine. "Poss" and "vis" are short enough for TTS.
- Decision: teacherScript and seg10 (say, subtitleEn) "so they take ible" -> "so they usually take ible"; tipJa and seg10 subtitleJa -> ～ ことが おおいよ (objective). seg8 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Can we check it? Then it's a fact. Is it a thought or a feeling? Then it's an opinion." (A+B).

#### L862 It's Just My Opinion
- A: The door-color poll, the Readers' Corner posts by Gus and Gale and "But it's a tasty one!" are natural. seg8 "It's just my opinion makes your words soft." is house quoting style (kept).
- B: The softener and "I respect your opinion" are well modeled; the quiz contrasts it with "That's terrible!". "Blue is the color of the sea and the sky" as a fact is fine for kids. Same -ible overclaim (Terr and horr). seg4 subtitleJa uses それとも in a plain list (Or a quiet, gentle voice); それとも is for questions.
- C: Reads well. "They end in ible" is understandable for TTS (single flag, kept).
- Decision: teacherScript "so they take ible" -> "so they usually take ible" and tipJa -> -ible に なる ことが おおいよ (objective). seg4 subtitleJa それとも -> または、 (objective JA).

#### L863 Is That True?
- A: The cake / lake rumor is a great hook and the Rumor Check story is natural. seg5 "A small paper with a short message" should be "a small piece of paper". The AI starter has Pip say "I heard a funny rumor", but the target expression is "Where did you read that?".
- B: seg4 "A safe place by the sea where boats stop and rest" is a relative clause in W29 (relative clauses are World 30 grammar). "Someone read lake as cake!" is hard to parse for kids. Same -ible overclaim (Incred and ed). Same starter flag. The note says "giant lake" and the team finds a "small blue lake"; the note exaggerated, so it is not a contradiction (kept).
- C: "Someone read lake as cake" has the read/read heteronym after a bare subject, and TTS may say "reed". The core "I read it on a note at the harbor" has the same risk, but it is a learning item (kept, reported below).
- Decision: teacherScript "Someone read lake as cake!" -> "Someone mixed up lake and cake!" (B+C) and "so they usually take ible" (objective). tipJa softened (objective JA). seg4 (say, subtitleEn) -> "A safe place by the sea. Boats stop and rest there." (B+C). seg5 (say, subtitleEn) -> "A small piece of paper" (A+B). starterEn "I heard a funny rumor. Anchor can fly at night!" -> "I found a funny note. It says Anchor can fly at night!" (A+B; frame kept).

#### L864 Ads and Opinions
- A: The Spinning Star ad, "Only five hundred shells" and "Be smart shoppers" are fun and natural. "round and round" is fine in US speech. seg0 subtitleJa かしこい おかいものの みんな is not natural Japanese.
- B: "Ads want to sell things, so they use big, happy words. Look for the facts first." is a good, kid-level media lesson. seg2 "Words and pictures that want you to buy something" and seg4 "A toy that spins round and round" are relative clauses in W29. Same -ible overclaim (Aud and respons). The drop-e rule for debatable is right.
- C: "pictures that want you to buy" is long for a vocab line. "Wheee!" is fine.
- Decision: teacherScript "so they usually take ible" and tipJa softened (objective). seg2 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Words and pictures. They want you to buy something." (B+C). seg4 (say, subtitleEn) -> "This toy spins round and round." (B+C). seg0 subtitleJa -> やあ、おかいもの じょうずな みんな！ (objective JA).

#### L865 Fact Check Team
- A: The report with the 100 vs 99 steps is a great example of checking a claim and finding it false. seg8 "For a fact, say how you checked it. In a book, or ask a grown-up." mixes a phrase and a verb.
- B: Good: a checkable sentence can still be false, and "That's an opinion, and that's OK." seg3 "A group that works together" and seg5 "Round, clear glass that makes light strong" are relative clauses in W29. ぼくも ぜんぜん おなじ 意見 (line 5, seg7 subtitleJa) uses ぜんぜん with a positive, which is slangy. Two layers (Gus asks in the teacherScript, Birimo as Pip asks in the conversation) do not contradict.
- C: "I TOTALLY agree" in capitals should be read as a word by TTS (single flag, kept). "Ible and able." is fine.
- Decision: seg3 (say, subtitleEn) -> "People work together as a group." (B+C). seg5 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Round, clear glass. It makes light strong." (B+C). seg8 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Look in a book, or ask a grown-up." (A+B). conversation line 5 ja and seg7 subtitleJa ぜんぜん -> まったく (objective JA).

#### L866 Mini Discussion: Homework
- A: The weekend-homework talk is balanced and natural ("Weekends are for rest, family, and playing outside."). seg2 "School work" should be one word in US spelling. "Riko is the chair" is used 24 times in W29 and taught with the class meeting (kept).
- B: Facts and opinions in the posts are sorted correctly (Gus practices spelling: fact; brains need a break: opinion). Birimo's "I partly agree" and Riko's "So you mean ...?" recycle earlier patterns well.
- C: teacherScript "Say STRONGLY strongly!" sounds like a stutter. seg1 ending on "I see your point, but." is fine.
- Decision: teacherScript "Say STRONGLY strongly!" -> "Make STRONGLY big!" (A+C). seg2 (say, subtitleEn) "School work" -> "Schoolwork" (objective).

#### L867 Mini Discussion: Pets at School
- A: Anchor's care card is warm and factual. Kiko's "Who can feed it in the summer vacation?" is not US usage (during summer vacation). "holiday" here means special days, which is fine in US English.
- B: seg2 "An animal that lives with you" is a relative clause in W29. seg9 "Verdict. ... It's the final answer." is not quite right; a verdict is a decision. "Two times a year" is fine for kids.
- C: seg10 "Make love big." is an unlucky collocation when heard. The rest reads well.
- Decision: conversation line 4 en -> "Who can feed it during summer vacation?" (A+B). seg2 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Pet. An animal friend at home, like a dog, a cat, or a hamster. Pet!" (B+C). seg9 (say, subtitleEn) -> "It's the final decision." with subtitleJa さいごに きまった こたえの ことだよ (A+B). seg10 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Make the word love big." (A+C).

#### L868 My Opinion Paragraph
- A: Riko's paragraph is exactly 60 words and has the opinion, two reasons, an example and the opinion again. Birimo's "it's… sweet!" joke works.
- B: The paragraph shape is taught clearly and the quiz checks the ending. "Make exactly strong" is understandable. Perspective and dictation explanations are right.
- C: seg10 "And is tiny, stop n think." is an invented respelling; TTS reads "n" as the letter "en". L866 and L867 use "tiny and quick" for the same idea.
- Decision: seg10 (say, subtitleEn) -> "And is tiny and quick." (B+C). The subtitleJa「ン」 stays.

#### L869 Lighthouse of Ideas Review
- A: The goodbye tour and tour card are natural and the tour order matches Kiko's plan (library first, lamp room last). "Tomorrow is the big discussion show" is inside Captain Pell's card (kept).
- B: The root explanations are right (contradict: say the opposite, contra = はんたいに). The card's "I'm sure the last lens will shine" is taught as an opinion (a hope); good.
- C: seg10 "Make so big." is slightly odd but reads right after "You are SO right!" (single flag, kept).
- Decision: no change.

#### L870 World 29 Grand Review
- A: The discussion show, vote and the six lenses are a satisfying finale. seg20 "Next, go and talk with Riko! Next is World 30!" repeats Next.
- B: Pip's Special Edition says "Birimo said, I see your point, but I have a different idea, and then he asked for cake", but in this lesson Kiko says that line (conversation, seg6) and Birimo says "Can I say something?" and asks for a small cake. "Tonight" is inside Pip's story (kept).
- C: "Make really big." reads like "make it really big" and is fine (single flag, kept). Speed TPR line is intact.
- Decision: teacherScript Pip's story -> "Birimo said, Can I say something? Then he asked for cake." (objective). seg20 (say, subtitleEn) "Next is World 30!" -> "World 30 is coming up!" (A+C).

#### Block-wide notes
- **-ible "not a word, so ible" rule**: stated as absolute in L861-864 (teacherScript, tipJa, L861 seg10); softened with "usually" / ことが おおい. If other groups (review lessons L866-870 or W30) restate it, use the same wording.
- **W29 relative clauses the regex misses**: the regex only lists people/something/a book etc. Grep W29 for `\b(a|an|the) \w+ (that|which|where) [a-z]+s?\b` in vocab-definition segments ("A toy that spins", "glass that makes", "an animal that lives", "a place where"). Contact clauses ("things you can read") are fine.
- **Heteronym read (past)**: the L863 core "I read it on a note at the harbor." may be read as "reed" by TTS after "I". It is a learning item, so I did not change it; if TTS tests show the problem, a pronunciation hint in the TTS layer is safer than changing the core.
- **Emphasis words in capitals** (TOTALLY, STRONGLY, LOVE, EXACTLY, SO, REALLY): all are real words, so TTS should say them as words; "Make X big" lines are fine except where the result is an unlucky phrase ("Make love big" in L867, fixed).
- Kept on purpose: "the chair" (W29 class meeting word), "holiday(s)" in the US sense in L867, "round and round" (L864), contact clauses, all "…" in conversation lines, the L861 JA hint, "Birimos are the cutest!", L863 "giant lake" vs "small blue lake" (the note exaggerated), story-internal "Tomorrow" / "Tonight" (L869, L870).

### Run 9 review notes: L871-880 (world 30, Stage 4)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide), plus testPoints. I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (US praise, I say / I ask, Hi / Hello openings, ノバ, "Let's pretend I'm" starters). There are 25 patches in 8 lessons; L874 and L878 have no change. There are no learning-item ID changes and no changes to school.expressions[].en, vocab[].en, tpr[].en or phonics words. Themes: (1) **Relative pronouns used before they are taught**, in vocab definitions that the test regex does not scan: "A machine that" / "A car that" in L871 (that starts in L872), "The person who" in L873 and "A person who" in L875 (who starts in L876). All are now plain sentences. (2) **"robot who"** in the L876 game plan and the L879 quiz card, which breaks the rule taught in L876 and L878 ("People get who. Things get that or which."); changed to "robot that". (3) **Small wording**: L872 "Is this a fact" with no clear "this"; L875 "kind words" vs JA ていねいな; L877 "robot repairer" (rare in US, hard to say) -> "robot mechanic"; L879 "astronaut is the most exciting job" (missing article), "Er and or mean" (TTS hesitation sound), "across the air"; L880 "Ten years later" for the future. Every new sentence has 12 words or fewer. The check script passes with ALL OK. All changed lessons are 209-226 s long with 100% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L871 Welcome to the City of Tomorrow
- A: The glass bridge, flying cars and Nova's Expo Pass are fun and natural. "the World 30 Invention Expo" sounds meta, but it is the event's name in L900 (expression and scene), so it stays. "Trees on the streets look like big solar panels" is a little odd but fits the story.
- B: L871 is one lesson before relative "that". seg3 "Robot. A machine that moves and works." and seg9 "Automobile. A car that moves by itself." use it early (the teacherScript already says "A car. It moves by itself."). The auto- = self tip and the four words are correct.
- C: Reads well. "Auto" alone is read as the word; fine.
- Decision: seg3 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Robot. A machine. It moves and works." and seg9 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Automobile. A car. It moves by itself." (objective, grammar order). JA unchanged (still matches).

#### L872 A Robot That Cleans
- A: Street Helper Number Seven and its sign are natural ("That makes my job easier."). In the teacherScript, "Is the robot safe for pets? Yes, because it moves slowly. Is this a fact or an opinion?" has no clear "this".
- B: Same flag; a fact question needs the exact sentence. The that + action point and the word-order quiz are clear. The JA hint （そうじする ロボット） is allowed here.
- C: "A robot, that, cleans." reads with good pauses.
- Decision: teacherScript -> "The sign says, I move slowly. Is that a fact or an opinion? It's a fact." (A+B).

#### L873 A Bus That Drives Itself
- A: The self-driving bus and the cat stop are realistic and safe (cameras, seat belts, never fast). "Bus. A big car for many people." is simple but fine (JA おおきな 車). "Hold the handle" for a strap is understood (single flag, kept).
- B: seg3 "Driver. The person who drives." uses who three lessons early. "Itself means by itself" is a clear point.
- C: Reads well.
- Decision: seg3 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Driver. A driver drives the bus or the car." (objective, grammar order; JA うんてん する ひと still fits).

#### L874 The Building Which Makes Energy
- A: The Sun Tower board (2,000 panels, 12 turbines, 300 homes) agrees with the lines. The title with "which" is pinned by tests; "that" is more common but "which" is the target.
- B: "For things, we can use that or which" is correct. The fact/opinion check ("amazing" = opinion) is right. tipJa "-ally で「〜に」" next to 自動で is a little loose (single flag, kept); the ワールド19 からくり工房 reference is real.
- C: "Wind. Moving air." reads as the noun; "wind turbine" fine.
- Decision: no change.

#### L875 City Tour Guide
- A: The duckling tour and Solar Tree Park sign are warm and natural. seg8 "A guide uses kind words" really means polite words (Please follow me).
- B: Same flag; JA says ていねいな ことば. seg3 "Guide. A person who shows you the way." uses who one lesson early (L876). The badge line is present.
- C: Reads well.
- Decision: seg3 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Guide. A guide shows you the way." (objective, grammar order). seg8 (say, subtitleEn) "kind words" -> "polite words" (A+B).

#### L876 A Person Who Designs Games
- A: Ms. Lin's studio and planning board are natural, and the break reminder is a nice health touch.
- B: The board says "a robot who builds bridges", but this lesson teaches "For people, we use who" and "Things get that or which". Objective contradiction. The trans- tip and words are correct.
- C: Reads well.
- Decision: teacherScript "a robot who builds bridges" -> "a robot that builds bridges" (objective).

#### L877 People Who Help Others
- A: The City Helper Center and poster are natural and kind. "robot repairer" is not a word US kids or teachers use; "robot mechanic" is. "collect cans and bottles, so they can be used again" sounds as if the workers are used again.
- B: Same flags (EN/JA stays: ロボットを なおす ひと). a person who / people who is clearly contrasted. transplant explained as moving a plant is right for kids.
- C: "repairer" (re-PAIR-er) is clumsy aloud. "Say thank you with a bow" could be read with bow = ribbon, but it is the same TPR line as W19, and "bow" after "a" in this context usually reads /baʊ/; kept (single flag).
- Decision: teacherScript (three "repairer(s)" and "so we can use them again"), conversation line 4, seg7 (say, subtitleEn, slide item) -> "robot mechanic(s)" (A+C).

#### L878 The Girl Who Invented It
- A: Tess's notebook (four tries, walked backward) is a lovely growth-mindset story; facts agree across lines.
- B: "Who plus an action tells us which girl" is correct. "Mistake. Something that goes wrong." uses that after L872; fine. tipJa -tion is fine.
- C: Reads well. Sprocket in the starter is not a child character, so the app is unaffected.
- Decision: no change.

#### L879 Who Am I?
- A: The quiz cards are fun. Riko's "I think astronaut is the most exciting job" misses an article. "Sending a signal across the air" should be "through the air".
- B: Quiz Card Four "I'm a robot who helps visitors" contradicts the who / that rule (objective). seg9 "Er and or mean a person or a thing." names suffixes as bare words. JA くうきの なかを とおして = through.
- C: TTS reads "Er" as a hesitation sound and "or" as the conjunction; the sentence is confusing aloud.
- Decision: teacherScript "robot who" -> "robot that", "astronaut is" -> "an astronaut has", "across the air" -> "through the air" (objective). seg9 (say, subtitleEn) -> "The ending E R or O R means a person or a thing." with JA おわりの er や or は (B+C). seg10 (say, subtitleEn) "across the air" -> "through the air" (A+B).

#### L880 Future Jobs
- A: The Future Job Fair poster and "cake tester" joke are natural. seg2 "Ten years later, you'll be big!" uses later (past-reference) for a future from now.
- B: Same flag; "In ten years" is the right frame next to will. "There will be means a guess about the future" is correct. Second badge is present.
- C: "you'll be all grown up" reads more smoothly than "you'll be big".
- Decision: seg2 (say, subtitleEn) -> "In ten years, you'll be all grown up!" (A+B; JA 10ねん たったら fits).

#### Block-wide notes
- **Relative pronouns in vocab definitions before their start lesson**: the test regex does not scan teacher.segments, so "A machine that", "A car that" (L871), "The person who" (L873), "A person who" (L875) slipped through. Please grep W29 (all relative clauses banned) and L871-875 segments for `(A|An|The) (person|machine|car|place|thing|robot|animal) (who|that|which) ` and `Someone who`.
- **"robot who"**: grep W30 for `robot who` / `robots who` (L876 and L879 had it); things take that / which, the rule the lessons teach.
- **"Ten years later" / "X later" for a future from now**: check other future-themed lessons (L891-900).
- **"Er and or" style bare suffixes**: check -ible / -able (W29), -ic (L881-884) for bare suffix sentences that TTS will read as words.
- Kept on purpose: "World 30 Invention Expo" (story name, L900 expression), "The Building Which Makes Energy" (tested title), "Say thank you with a bow" (W19 TPR line too), L873 "A big car for many people", L874 tipJa wording, "Beep!" for Nova, all "…" lines.

### Run 9 review notes: L881-890 (world 30, Stage 4)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide), plus testPoints. I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (US praise, I say / I ask, Hi / Hello openings, ノバ, "Let's pretend I'm" starters). There are 54 patches in 8 lessons; L886 and L889 have no change. There are no learning-item ID changes and no changes to school.expressions[].en, vocab[].en, tpr[].en or phonics words. The relative clauses (that / who) are used at or after their start lessons, "One good point is" / "One problem is" first appear in L886, "Think before you post" in L888 and "However," in L890. The online safety (no address / phone / school name, never meet internet strangers, tell a grown-up) and the AI facts (AI can sound sure when it is wrong, check in two places) are correct. Themes: (1) **Comma-split -ic beats**: the clap segments in L881-884 and L890 split words with commas ("e, LEC, tric", "sci, en, TIF, ic"), so TTS reads each part as its own word and may misread bare "ic," / "sci," / "re,". I changed them to the hyphen beat form (e-LEC-tric), the same form as the app's -ic quiz choices, in say, subtitleEn and subtitleJa (27 patches). (2) **Story logic**: in L884 Tess said "so we changed the design" before the change, and "This time, it worked!" came with no second test; in L885 Mr. Shell and Tess were judges of their own invention while Mr. Shell walked in the demo. (3) **Small wording**: "with that" (L882), "the careful side" (L887), "Once you post, ... see it" (L888), "Riko is the chair" and "more fun than robots" (L890), the speed TPR "Pass up!" (L885, L890), and a confusing JA "かつ ひと" (L885). Every new sentence has 15 words or fewer. The check script passes with ALL OK. All changed lessons are 216-224 s long with 100% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L881 What's the Problem?
- A: Hill Street, Mr. Shell and Tess's Problem Notebook are natural, and "Inventors always start with a problem" is a good frame. seg3 "A hill that goes up, up, up, very fast" mixes steepness with speed (single flag, kept; the gesture makes it clear).
- B: "The problem is that + whole sentence" is clear, and the quiz (he walks fine without bags, so the bags are the problem) checks the reasoning. The fact check of the notebook (Tess counted) fits W29-30. The -ic stress rule is correct (e-LEC-tric, ro-BOT-ic, sci-en-TIF-ic, fan-TAS-tic).
- C: seg9 and seg10 split syllables with commas; "ic," and "sci," alone are TTS risks, and "e," may be read as the letter name. "LEC is strong" is the house capital-stress style (kept).
- Decision: seg9, seg10 (say, subtitleEn, subtitleJa) comma beats -> hyphen beat form (B+C).

#### L882 An Idea That Helps
- A: The idea board is warm and realistic ("Every idea is welcome", "Small ideas can help a lot"). vocab "lift" is the verb, fine in US English.
- B: "A bag. What kind of bag? A bag that follows you." is a clean way to show what the relative clause adds. seg1 "Explain your idea with that" and seg21 "share an idea with that" sound like "using that thing"; the JA says that を つかって.
- C: teacherScript "Futuristic. IS is strong." is read "is is strong", which trips the voice and the listener. seg10 and seg11 have the comma beats.
- Decision: seg1 and seg21 (say, subtitleEn) "with that" -> "with the word that" (B+C). teacherScript "IS is strong" -> "fu-tur-IS-tic." (B+C). seg10, seg11 (say, subtitleEn, subtitleJa) -> hyphen beat form (B+C).

#### L883 How Does It Work?
- A: The design card (wrist band, sensors, solar panel on the lid) is clear, and "Well, it's a plan. We haven't tested it yet!" is a nice fact / plan contrast. seg6 "Birimo floats over" copies Nova (kept).
- B: "It uses ~ to ~" is explained as tool + job; the rice cooker model is good. The -ic words (ar-TIS-tic, ath-LET-ic, dra-MAT-ic, ter-RIF-ic) are stressed correctly.
- C: seg9 and seg10 comma beats; otherwise reads well.
- Decision: seg9, seg10 (say, subtitleEn, subtitleJa) -> hyphen beat form (B+C).

#### L884 Test and Improve
- A: The cat and the stairs are a fun, believable failure, and the test report is a good model. In the teacherScript Tess says "It didn't work well, so we changed the design" and only after that "We turn off the camera and put on bigger wheels"; then "Let's try again. This time, it worked!" follows with no second test.
- B: Same flag: the past tense "changed" must come after the change, or the frame teaches the wrong time order. The -ic words are correct. The teacherScript "Realistic. LIS is strong." names the stressed sound (/ˈlɪs/), while the quiz and slides use re-al-IS-tic; not wrong, so kept (single flag). seg20 "they turned off the camera" vs "we" in the story (single flag, kept).
- C: seg10, seg11 comma beats ("re," may be read "R E").
- Decision: teacherScript reordered: the change first, then "Tess says, It didn't work well, so we changed the design.", and "Mr. Shell walks up the hill, and the bag follows him all the way." before "This time, it worked!" (A+B). seg10, seg11 (say, subtitleEn, subtitleJa) -> hyphen beat form (B+C).

#### L885 Invention Pitch
- A: The pitch is a good model (problem, idea, test, thank you). But "The judges are Nova, Tess, and Mr. Shell": Mr. Shell walks in our demo and Tess helped build the bag, so they judge their own invention. Speed TPR "Pass up!" means to turn something down.
- B: Same two flags. seg3 subtitleJa "よく きいて、かつ ひとを えらぶ ひと" reads as "and also, a person"; the meaning (who wins) is unclear. "We tested it two times" is fine for kids (twice would also do; kept).
- C: Reads well. "Electric. LEC is strong." is house style. "Pass up!" is said fast and sounds like a different command.
- Decision: teacherScript judges -> "The judges are Nova and Ms. Lin." (A+B; Ms. Lin is in the W30 cast, and Nova is still the judge in the starter). seg3 subtitleJa -> "よく きいて、だれが かちかを きめる ひと。" (objective JA). seg14 (say, subtitleEn) "Pass up!" -> "Hold it up!" (A+B).

#### L886 Good Points and Problems
- A: The Robot Station sign is balanced and true (robots never tire and do dangerous jobs; they break, need energy and can't understand feelings like friends; people make the big decisions).
- B: "One good point is that ~. One problem is that ~." first appears here (tested order). The letter-name rule for AI, USB, TV, PC is correct; "It means artificial intelligence" is a helpful gloss.
- C: "A, I. AI." reads well. "Beep, boop!" is fine.
- Decision: no change.

#### L887 Screens Everywhere
- A: The poster (20-20-20 eye breaks, arm's length, screens off an hour before bed, play outside) is accurate and not preachy; Nova's "It's different for every family" is good. seg1 "Say the good side and the careful side" and seg9 "That's the careful side" are not natural English.
- B: Same flag; the JA (気を つける ところ) says "something to be careful about". The unit rule (say the whole word, plural s) is correct.
- C: The teacherScript "km. We say kilometers. ... mm. We say millimeters." has bare abbreviations; TTS may hum "mm" (single flag; the segments already use "This one is ~", kept and noted below).
- Decision: seg1 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Say a good point and something to be careful about." and seg9 (say, subtitleEn) -> "That's something to be careful about." (A+B).

#### L888 Safe and Smart Online
- A: The house-number photo, the Kids Board rules and "Write only things you would say to someone's face" are real and age-right. seg9 "Once you post, many people can see it" has no noun for "it".
- B: Same flag (the rule card says "Once you post something"). Safety content is correct and complete. "3D ... you can see something from every side" is a loose but kid-level gloss (kept). The JA hint Post（とうこうする） is allowed here.
- C: Reads well. "three D" is spelled as words, good for TTS.
- Decision: seg9 (say, subtitleEn) "Once you post," -> "Once you post something," (A+B).

#### L889 AI Helpers
- A: The six-legs mistake checked in a book is a strong, true example; the sign ("it can sound sure even when it is wrong", check in two places, ask a librarian) is accurate. seg6 "Kiko does spider homework" is plain kid narration (kept).
- B: "AI can help us, but we should check the answers" plus "AI can make mistakes, too" is the right message. NASA / STEM as words and ID / DIY as letters are correct; STEM and DIY are glossed.
- C: Reads well. "Even me!" is natural spoken English for Birimo playing Nova.
- Decision: no change.

#### L890 Technology Talk
- A: The talk is a good review of opinion + reason + However. "Riko is the chair" is formal and, for kids, sounds like a seat. Birimo's "playing outside is more fun than robots" compares an activity with things. Speed TPR "Pass up!" as in L885.
- B: Same flags (the JA says しかい). "However, then a worry" and the because / however quiz are clear. "Is the group's idea a fact or an opinion? It's an opinion." is correct.
- C: seg9 "ro, BOT, ic." comma beats.
- Decision: teacherScript and seg6 (say, subtitleEn) "Riko is the chair." -> "Riko leads the talk." (A+B). "more fun than robots" -> "more fun than playing with robots" in the teacherScript, conversation line 3 (en, ja ロボットで あそぶより), seg7 (say, subtitleEn, subtitleJa) and seg7 slide item (A+B). seg13 (say, subtitleEn) "Pass up!" -> "Hold it up!" (A+B). seg9 (say, subtitleEn, subtitleJa) -> hyphen beat form (B+C).

#### Block-wide notes
- **Comma-split stress beats**: fixed in L881-884 and L890 as the hyphen beat form. Please grep the W30 review lessons (L895, L900) and the W29 stress lessons for `\b[a-z]+, [A-Z]{2,}, [a-z]+\.` and the JA `、[A-Z]{2,}、`.
- **"X is strong" with X = IS**: "IS is strong" is hard to say and hear; check any later review of futuristic / realistic.
- **LIS vs IS**: L884 teacherScript says "Realistic. LIS is strong." (the sound), while the quiz shows re-al-IS-tic (the spelling split). Not wrong; pick one if you want full consistency.
- **Bare "ic"**: "Words that end in ic" / "before ic" appear in every -ic lesson. If the TTS says "I C" instead of "ick", change only the spoken form; the focus label "-ic" is pinned.
- **Speed TPR "Pass up!"** (L885, L890): grep W30 (L895, L900 badges) for `Pass up!`.
- **Bare unit abbreviations in teacherScript** (L887 "km. ... mm."): fine if the teacherScript is not read by TTS; otherwise use "This one is kilometers" as the segments do.
- Kept on purpose: L881 seg3 "very fast" for steep; L883 "Birimo floats over"; L884 seg20 "they turned off"; L885 "two times"; L888 3D gloss; L889 "Kiko does spider homework" and "Even me!"; capital stress words (LEC, TAS, BOT) as the house style; Nova as "she".

### Run 9 review notes: L891-900 (world 30, Stage 4)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide), plus testPoints. I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (US praise, I say / I ask, Hi / Hello openings, ノバ, "Let's pretend I'm" starters). There are 39 patches in 10 lessons; every lesson has at least one change. There are no learning-item ID changes and no changes to school.expressions[].en, vocab[].en, tpr[].en or phonics words. Casual forms (gonna, wanna ...) stay only after "Now, phonics." in the L891-895 teacherScripts; segments and the L896+ conversations use them as taught. The relative clauses (that / which / who) are the grammar of the world and are kept. Themes: (1) **Syllable spell-outs for TTS**: the -ic review lines wrote "Fu, tu, RIS, tic", "E, LEC, tric", "Sci, en, TIF, ic", "Mag, NET, ic" with commas, and "Clap the beat before ic!" has a bare "ic". I changed these to the hyphen beat form the brief asks for (fu-tu-RIS-tic, e-LEC-tric, sci-en-TIF-ic, mag-NET-ic) and named the letters "I C". (2) **Wrong sound in L898**: "En, er, GET, ic. Clap on get!" teaches a hard g, but the g in energetic is soft; changed to en-er-JET-ic / "Clap on jet!" (the JA already said ジェット). (3) **The word "that" heard as a pronoun** (L893 point and answer, "Put that in the middle", "That tells us the job"); now "the word that". (4) **Story contradiction in L895**: the teacherScript had Nova ask and Kiko answer, but the conversation and seg7 have Birimo ask Nova and Nova answer; Kiko's line quoted Nova ("Nova says, ...") instead of using the block's role-play frame. (5) Small wording: "sicknesses" (L891), "Then but adds" (L892), the missing "after if, use not will use" rule (L894), "headline" (L897), lowercase after a quoting comma (L898), "Next ... And next" (L900). Every new sentence has 12 words or fewer. The check script passes with ALL OK. All lessons are 219-228 s long with 100% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L891 In 2050…
- A: Tomorrow Hall, the prediction screens and "a smart guess with good reasons" are natural. The reading line "New medicine can stop many sicknesses early." uses a rare plural; a US teacher says illnesses. seg9 "Now, fast friend talk!" is a little clipped (single flag, kept).
- B: "will probably" first appears here (tested). "Probably means a strong maybe" and the "A prediction is not a fact" quiz are correct and kind (every idea is welcome). gonna = going to and wanna = want to, with "write the full words", are correct. EN/JA match.
- C: "sicknesses" is a run of s sounds and hard to say. "In twenty fifty" is written out in say, good for TTS.
- Decision: teacherScript "sicknesses" -> "illnesses" (A+C).

#### L892 Schools in the Future
- A: The future classroom, Birimo's four-hour lunch and "Friends and teachers help us learn and grow" are warm and natural. "at their own speed" could be "at their own pace" (single flag, kept).
- B: "Will is a strong guess. Might is a small maybe." is a clear contrast. seg8 "Then but adds your feeling." is hard to parse because the word but is used as a subject.
- C: "Then but adds" sounds like a broken sentence aloud. "You're kinda amazing. No, really amazing!" is a fun casual model in a segment (kept).
- Decision: seg8 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Then add your feeling with but." (A+C).

#### L893 Homes in the Future
- A: Riko's design card (roof garden, rainwater tank, reading room) is natural. "Tank. A big box that holds water." is a bit loose, but box is kid-level and the JA says いれもの (single flag, kept).
- B: The relative "that" point is right, but "Put that in the middle ... That tells us the job." does not say it is the word that; a child hears "put that (thing)". The quiz answer "We need that before the job" has the same problem.
- C: With no quotation marks in say, TTS reads "that" as a normal pronoun in both lines.
- Decision: seg8 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Put the word that in the middle. Windows that make energy. The job comes after it." (B+C). seg19 (say, subtitleEn) -> "We need the word that before the job." (B+C). JA already matches.

#### L894 If We Use Clean Energy
- A: The two model cities, the energy-saving tips (turn off lights, close the fridge quickly, walk or bike for short trips) and "wind works at night, too" are correct and natural. A teacher teaching the first conditional always says "after if, use the present".
- B: seg8 names if and will but never states the rule that the if part takes the present ("If we use", not "If we will use"), which is the classic learner error at this point.
- C: "If is the first step" and "Then will is what happens next" read fine. "Wind" (noun) is unambiguous in context.
- Decision: seg8 (say, subtitleEn) add "After if, say use, not will use." with JA if の あとは will use では なくて use だよ。(A+B).

#### L895 Future City Plan
- A: The presentation, the plan poster ("One problem is that it will cost a lot of money. So we will build the city little by little") and "Everyone should feel welcome" are natural. In the conversation, Birimo asks Nova a question and Kiko answers with "Nova says, ...", which is odd.
- B: The teacherScript says "Nova asks, What will there be for older people? Kiko answers, There will be benches...", but the conversation and seg7 have Birimo ask Nova and Nova answer: a story contradiction between layers. The block uses "I'm playing Nova!" for children speaking as Nova. The review tip for USB, transport and automatic is correct.
- C: "Fu, tu, RIS, tic" with commas and capitals, and the bare "ic" in "Clap the beat before ic!", are TTS risks.
- Decision: teacherScript -> "Then he asks, Nova, what will there be in your future city? Nova answers, There will be benches and gentle hills for older people." and "fu-tu-RIS-tic" (objective). conversation.lines[4] (en, ja) and seg7 slide item -> "I'm playing Nova! Beep! There will be benches and gentle hills for older people." (A+B). seg10 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Fu-tu-RIS-tic. Clap the beat just before the I C ending!" (B+C).

#### L896 Gonna, Wanna
- A: Tess's lab note ("In a letter, a report, or a test, please use the full words") and Haru's "I'll write going to in my report" are natural and well judged. The note lists four casual forms but names only going to and want to as written forms (single flag, kept).
- B: The speak/write distinction is clear and correct. "Trans means change" for transform differs from "across" elsewhere, but w30Phonics.ts glosses trans- as むこうへ・かわる, so it is consistent with the table (kept).
- C: "Say e, LEC, tric" / "E, LEC, tric" may be read as letters; the brief's example is e-LEC-tric.
- Decision: teacherScript and seg10 (say, subtitleEn) -> "e-LEC-tric" (B+C).

#### L897 Reading a Tech Article
- A: The Rooty article is natural news English, and "It's a fact that some people feel this way" handles the worry line well. seg16 "Make your own headline" is followed by a full sentence, not a headline.
- B: Main idea vs details and fact vs opinion ("I think tells us it's an opinion") are correct. The seg16 JA says じぶんの きじを かんがえて, which does not match "headline".
- C: "Sci, en, TIF, ic" has a bare "ic" and capitals.
- Decision: teacherScript and seg10 (say, subtitleEn) -> "sci-en-TIF-ic" (B+C). seg16 (say, subtitleEn) "Make your own headline." -> "Think of your own article." (A+B).

#### L898 My Invention Proposal
- A: The Spell Pencil proposal (it does not fix mistakes for you, so you learn; cheap parts, shared pencils) is natural and well shaped. seg2 "A paper that says, here is my idea." breaks the house capital-after-comma style.
- B: The proposal checklist (name, job, how it works, who it helps, one problem) is clear. "En, er, GET, ic. Clap on get!" teaches the wrong sound: the g in energetic is soft (/dʒ/), and the JA already writes ジェット.
- C: TTS reads "GET" and "get" with a hard g. STEM is read as one word, correctly.
- Decision: teacherScript -> "en-er-JET-ic", seg10 (say, subtitleEn) -> "En-er-JET-ic. Clap on jet!" and JA "ジェット で" (objective). seg2 (say, subtitleEn) "here" -> "Here" (objective, house style).

#### L899 City of Tomorrow Review
- A: The goodbye tour, the watering robot and the clock door sign are natural, and they set up the Corridor of Time well. "Hallway. A long way inside a building" is fine for kids.
- B: "A thing? Use that. A person? Use who." and the who / which quiz are correct. The transplant gloss (move a plant to a new place) is right.
- C: "Mag, NET, ic" has a bare "ic" and capitals.
- Decision: teacherScript and seg10 (say, subtitleEn) -> "mag-NET-ic" (B+C).

#### L900 World 30 Grand Review
- A: The Expo, the six badges, "The future is not something we just wait for" and the opening clock door make a strong finale. seg20 says "Next, go and talk with Birimo! And next is World thirty-one", which repeats next.
- B: The quiz "something, blank, we create together, who or that" and "The future is a thing, not a person" are right. "It's an opinion, and a good one!" treats opinion kindly. In the teacherScript Nova congratulates; in the conversation Ms. Emma does, and seg7 follows the teacherScript. Nova cannot be a conversation speaker, so this is fine (kept).
- C: "Fu, tu, RIS, tic" has the same TTS risk as L895.
- Decision: teacherScript and seg10 (say, subtitleEn) -> "fu-tu-RIS-tic" (B+C). seg20 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Then it's on to World thirty-one, the Corridor of Time!" with JA それから ワールド31、ときの回廊へ！ (A+C).

#### Block-wide notes
- **-ic syllable spell-outs**: the comma-and-capitals form ("Fu, tu, RIS, tic", "Say e, LEC, tric") and the bare "before ic" lines ("Clap the beat before ic!", "The strong beat comes before ic.", "Say the part just before ic strongly.") appear across L881-900 in world-30.json. I fixed only L891-900. The pattern to fix elsewhere is `[A-Z][a-z]*, [a-z]+, [A-Z]{2,}, [a-z]+` and `before ic\b`.
- **Soft g / soft c in stressed syllables**: check any other -ic spell-out whose stressed letters do not match the sound (energetic GET = jet). Similar cases could be ma-GIC-type words or "-gic" / "-cic" endings in L881-884 if they are spelled out.
- **"that" as a word vs pronoun** in relative-clause point segments: say has no quotation marks, so "Put that in the middle" is heard as a pronoun. "the word that" is safer. This may recur in L872-875 point segments.
- Kept on purpose: "…" in the title and in "Beep…" (L899), "Beep!", story-internal "tomorrow" (L899 Ms. Emma), the Nova "she" pronoun (matches L871), "You're kinda amazing" and "gonna be great presenters" in segments (casual forms after the lesson that teaches them), "Trans means change" for transform (matches the w30Phonics.ts gloss むこうへ・かわる), "Usb" as the wrong option in the L895 quiz.
- Single flags (not changed): L891 "fast friend talk", L892 "at their own speed" (pace), L893 tank as "a big box", L896 the note naming only going to / want to as written forms.

| 846 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, I think reading is fun. One reason is that books take us to new places. For example, I visited a dragon island in a story. Today, Captain Pell opens a round wooden door. Wow! It's the lighthouse library! There are books about the sea, the stars, and faraway islands. Kiko says, Reading is fun! Haru says, I agree with you. Reading is fun. Say it with me. I agree with you. Reading is fun. Great! When you have the same idea as your friend, say, I agree with you. Riko asks, Do you agree with me? Books are better at night. Birimo says, Yes, I do. Books and snacks are better at night! Kiko finds a big blue book and says, Me too! I love stories about the sea. Captain Pell laughs. He says, When friends agree, they still listen. They say why. Let's read the poster on the library door. "Book Talk Time in the Lighthouse Library. Every Friday evening, bring your favorite book. Tell your friends about it. Say why you like it. Listen when your friends talk. Say, I agree with you, when you have the same idea. Ask a question when you want to know more. You can borrow two books at a time. Please bring them back in one week. Snacks are OK, but please keep the books clean! From Captain Pell." When is Book Talk Time? Every Friday evening. Are snacks OK? Yes, but keep the books clean. Now, phonics. Today's new word part is dict. Dict means say. Predict. Pre means before. You say it before it happens. Dictionary. A book of words. Let's look it up in the dictionary! Contradict. Contra means against. You say the opposite. Let's find a good book together! | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, I think reading is fun. | 847 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, I agree with you. Reading is fun. Today, we hear loud voices outside. Two seagull brothers are on the rocks. Their names are Gus and Gale. Gus shouts, The mountains are more fun! Gale shouts, No! The beach is more fun! They don't listen to each other. Oh no! The light gets weaker. Captain Pell sighs. Ms. Emma says, Let's try a kind way to answer. First, show you heard your friend. Then, give your own idea. Gus tries first. I think the mountains are more fun because we can hike. Gale takes a deep breath. He says, I see your point, but I think the beach is more fun. Say it with me. I see your point, but I think the beach is more fun. Wonderful! Gus is surprised. He says, Oh. Thank you for listening. Captain Pell smiles and says, Let's listen to each other. Let's read Gus and Gale's notes on the lighthouse board. "Gus: I think the mountains are more fun because we can hike and see beautiful views. The air is cool and fresh, too. Gale: I see your point, but I think the beach is more fun. We can swim, build sandcastles, and play in the waves. The sound of the sea is relaxing, and the sand feels warm under our feet. Captain Pell: Both ideas are good. Mountains and beaches are both special places. Thank you for writing kindly!" Why does Gus like the mountains? Because he can hike and see beautiful views. What can Gale do at the beach? Swim, build sandcastles, and play in the waves. Now, phonics. Dict means say. Prediction. A guess about the future. My prediction is rain tomorrow. Dictation. Your teacher says words, and you write them down. Verdict. The final decision. Let's listen first and speak kindly! | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, I agree with you. Reading is fun. | 850 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, I partly agree. Summer is fun, but it's too hot. Today, Captain Pell starts the Agree or Disagree Game! Everybody comes to the beach below the lighthouse. Gus and Gale come, too. Captain Pell reads a sentence. Summer is the best season. If you agree, stand on the left. If you disagree, stand on the right. Then give a reason. Haru stands on the left. He says, I agree because summer vacation is long. Riko stands on the right. She says, I disagree because winter is my favorite season. Say it with me. I disagree because winter is my favorite season. Great! Riko loves skating and hot soup. Gus says, I see your point, Riko. Gale says, Both ideas are good! Nobody shouts. Everyone listens. Then the brothers play too, and they take turns kindly. Suddenly, the second lens begins to shine! Now two lenses are lit! Let's read the game card. "The Agree or Disagree Game. One: Listen to the sentence. Two: Choose a side. Stand on the left for agree. Stand on the right for disagree. Three: Say, I agree because, or, I disagree because, and give a reason. Four: Listen to the other side. Five: Clap for every idea. Six: Say thank you to the other team. Remember, there is no wrong side. Every reason is welcome. Kind words make the light shine!" Where do you stand to disagree? On the right. What should you do for every idea? Clap for it. Now, phonics. Let's review dict and spect. Predict. Say before. Contradict. Say against. Respectful. You show respect. Perspective. Your way of seeing things. You're all wonderful players. | 855 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, Let's vote. Most people chose the park. Today, Ms. Emma has a plan. She says, Back home, we will have a real class meeting soon. Excellent! Riko says, I'd like to go to the aquarium because I love sea animals. Haru says, Let's hear from Kiko. Kiko says, I think the science museum is better. We can try fun experiments. Then Birimo raises his hand. Can I say something? Haru says, Sure, go ahead. Birimo says, The farm! They have fresh ice cream! Everyone laughs. Then Haru says, Let's vote. Everyone votes, and Haru says, Most people chose the aquarium. Suddenly, the third lens begins to shine! Captain Pell says, Wonderful! You asked, you listened, and you decided together. Let's read Kiko's meeting notes. "Class Meeting Notes. Topic: our school trip. Chair: Haru. Ideas: the aquarium, the science museum, and the farm. Riko likes the aquarium because she loves sea animals. Kiko likes the science museum because of the experiments. Birimo likes the farm because of the ice cream. Vote: the aquarium, five. The science museum, three. The farm, two. Decision: the aquarium. Everyone listened to each other, and nobody shouted. Birimo was a little sad about the ice cream, but he clapped for the aquarium. Next time, the science museum!" Who was the chair? Haru. Why does Kiko like the science museum? Because of the experiments. Now, phonics. I really think so. I REALLY think so. I do agree with you.
