| 856 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, Today's topic is our school trip. What do you think? Three lenses are shining now! Today, we climb the stairs to the lamp room at the top. Gale looks at the new lens and says, Wow, it's so cool! Birimo touches it. Hmm, it isn't cold. It's warm! Kiko asks Gale, What do you mean by "cool"? Say it with me. What do you mean by "cool"? Nice! Gale laughs. Cool can mean a little cold. But here, it means great. Birimo says, Oh, it means great! Gale says, Right. Then Captain Pell says, Now, let's go down to the galley for some tea. Haru asks, What does galley mean? Captain Pell says, It means a kitchen on a ship. My lighthouse is like a big ship! When you don't know a word, just ask. It isn't rude. It helps everyone understand. Let's read the list on the galley wall. "Captain Pell's Sailor Words. Galley means a kitchen on a ship. Deck means the floor of a ship. Ahoy means hello, and it is fun to shout! Aye aye means yes, I understand. Bow means the front of a ship, and stern means the back. Shipshape means neat and clean. Sailors use these words every day. Some of them sound strange, but they are easy to learn. Ask me about any word, any time. I love questions!" What does deck mean? The floor of a ship. How do sailors say hello? Ahoy! Now, phonics. In fast speech, can is short and weak. It sounds like kun. I can help you. We can do it together. Can you say that again? You can sit here. When you hear a new word, ask, What do you mean? Have fun with new words! | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, Today's topic is our school trip. What do you think? Three lenses are shining now! Today, we climb the stairs to the lamp room at the top. Gale looks at the new lens and says, Wow, it's so cool! Birimo touches it. Hmm, it isn't cold. It's warm! Kiko asks Gale, What do you mean by "cool"? Say it with me. What do you mean by "cool"? Nice! Gale laughs. Cool can mean a little cold. But here, it means great. Birimo says, Oh, it means great! Gale says, Right. Then Captain Pell says, Now, let's go down to the galley for some tea. Haru asks, What does galley mean? Captain Pell says, It means a kitchen on a ship. My lighthouse is like a big ship! When you don't know a word, just ask. It isn't rude. It helps everyone understand. Let's read the list on the galley wall. "Captain Pell's Sailor Words. Galley means a kitchen on a ship. Deck means the floor of a ship. Ahoy means hello, and it is fun to shout! Aye aye means yes, I understand. Stern means the back of a ship. Shipshape means neat and clean. Sailors use these words every day. Some of them sound strange, but they are easy to learn. Ask me about any word, any time. I love questions!" What does deck mean? The floor of a ship. How do sailors say hello? Ahoy! Now, phonics. In fast speech, can is short and weak. It sounds like kun. I can help you. We can do it together. Can you say that again? You can sit here. When you hear a new word, ask, What do you mean? Have fun with new words! | B+C | heteronym bow (bau / boh, also ribbon and greeting); TTS and kids may misread it |

| 861 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, Why do you think so? Can you tell me more? Today, Captain Pell takes us up a narrow staircase. Look at the lamp! Four lenses are shining now. Only two lenses are still dark. At the top of the stairs, there is a small, busy room. It's the newsroom! A young puffin flies in with a pencil behind her ear. Her name is Pip. She is a reporter, and she makes The Lighthouse News. Pip says, A good newspaper needs facts. A fact（じじつ） is true, and we can check it. An opinion is an idea or a feeling. Other people can feel differently. Pip holds up a picture of a sleeping cat. "Cats sleep a lot" is a fact. Say it with me. "Cats sleep a lot" is a fact. Great! Then Pip says, "Cats are the cutest animals" is an opinion. Birimo says, Is this a fact or an opinion? Birimos are the cutest! Pip laughs. It's an opinion, Birimo! Let's read Pip's news story. "The Lighthouse News, Page Two. Captain Pell has lived in the lighthouse for forty years. The lighthouse has six lenses, and four of them are shining now. Anchor, the captain's dog, waits at the door for him every day. In my opinion, Anchor is the sweetest dog on the island. Last night, three little boats came home safely because of the light. I think the light is the most beautiful thing in the sea of clouds. By Pip, Reporter" How many lenses are shining? Four. Anchor is the sweetest dog on the island. Is this a fact or an opinion? It's an opinion. The lighthouse has six lenses. Is this a fact or an opinion? It's a fact. We can count them! Now, phonics. Possible. Visible. Reasonable. Acceptable. Reason plus able makes reasonable. Accept plus able makes acceptable. Poss and vis are not words by themselves, so they take ible. Let's find more facts, reporters! | Hello, everyone! "Cats sleep a lot" is a fact. Say it with me. "Cats sleep a lot" is a fact. Great! Then Pip says, "Cats are the cutest animals" is an opinion. Birimo says, Is this a fact or an opinion? It's just my opinion, but I think green is the best color. Lovely! Pip says, Those words make your opinion soft and kind. Other people can still share their ideas. Haru says, I respect your opinion. I like blue. Birimo shouts, Brown is the best color, because it looks like chocolate cake! Riko asks, Is that a fact? Birimo grins. No, it's just my opinion. Let's read two posts from the Readers' Corner. "Readers' Corner. Gus says: It's just my opinion, but blue is the best color for the door. Blue is the color of the sea and the sky. It makes me feel calm. Gale says: I respect my brother's opinion, but I think yellow is the best. Yellow is bright and cheerful, like the morning sun. Little boats can see a yellow door from far away. Both of us love the newsroom, so please vote!" What color does Gale like? Yellow. Blue is the color of the sea and the sky. Is this a fact or an opinion? It's a fact. Yellow is cheerful. Is this a fact or an opinion? It's an opinion. Gus and Gale don't argue now. They listen to each other. Now, phonics. Terrible. Horrible. These are strong words, so be careful with them in a discussion! Suitable. Suit plus able. Understandable. Understand plus able. | 863 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, It's just my opinion, but I think green is the best color. Today, Birimo runs into the newsroom. Big news! There's a giant cake under the lighthouse! Everyone stops. Riko asks two smart questions. Is that true? Where did you read that? Say it with me. Is that true? Where did you read that? Excellent! Birimo says, I read it on a note at the harbor. Haru says, Let's check it together. Kiko says, Good idea! Pip grabs her notebook. A good reporter always checks the news. So the friends walk down to the harbor and find the note. Then they walk behind the lighthouse. What do they find? Let's read Pip's story. "Rumor Check, by Pip. This week, a funny rumor went around the island. A giant cake is hidden under the lighthouse! I found the first note at the harbor. It said, There is a giant lake behind the lighthouse. Someone read lake as cake! Our team walked behind the lighthouse and checked. There is a small blue lake there. There is no cake. Before you share news, please ask two questions. Is that true? Where did you read that?" Where was the first note? At the harbor. What is behind the lighthouse? A small blue lake. There is a small blue lake behind the lighthouse. Is this a fact or an opinion? It's a fact. The team checked it with their own eyes. Birimo looks sad. I think a cake is better than a lake! Haru laughs. I see your point, but a lake is great for little boats. Now, phonics. Incredible. Edible. Edible means you can eat it. Is the lake edible? No, Birimo! Remarkable. Remark plus able. Agreeable. Agree plus able. Incred and ed are not words by themselves, so they take ible. Always check the news, friends! | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, It's just my opinion, but I think green is the best color. "Rumor Check, by Pip. This week, a funny rumor went around the island. A giant cake is hidden under the lighthouse! I found the first note at the harbor. It said, There is a giant lake behind the lighthouse. Someone mixed up lake and cake! Our team walked behind the lighthouse and checked. There is a small blue lake there. There is no cake. Before you share news, please ask two questions. Is that true? Where did you read that?" Where was the first note? At the harbor. What is behind the lighthouse? A small blue lake. There is a small blue lake behind the lighthouse. Is this a fact or an opinion? It's a fact. The team checked it with their own eyes. Birimo looks sad. I think a cake is better than a lake! Haru laughs. I see your point, but a lake is great for little boats. Now, phonics. Incredible. Edible. Edible means you can eat it. Is the lake edible? No, Birimo! Remarkable. Remark plus able. Agreeable. Agree plus able. Incred and ed are not words by themselves, so they usually take ible. Always check the news, friends! | B+C | "Someone read lake as cake" is hard for kids and TTS may say read as reed; mixed up is simple. Plus the -ible rule softened (objective) |

| 864 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, Is that true? Where did you read that? Today, Pip is working on the last page of The Lighthouse News. It's the ad page! A shop at the harbor wants to sell a new toy. It's a spinning top, and it looks like a star. The ad has very big words. Haru reads it and frowns. "This is the best toy ever!" Is it a fact? Say it with me. "This is the best toy ever!" Is it a fact? Good thinking! Riko says, No, we can't check it. Kiko points at another line. "It spins for three minutes." We can check that with a clock. Birimo jumps up. I'm playing the toy seller! Everyone loves it! Haru smiles. Hmm, that's an opinion. Let's read the ad together. "NEW! The Spinning Star! This is the best toy ever! It spins for three minutes. It glows in the dark, so you can play at night. It is light and easy to carry, so you can take it to the beach or the park. It comes in five colors. Everyone loves it! You will be happy all day long! Only five hundred shells at the Harbor Shop. Hurry, before they are all gone!" How many colors does it come in? Five. It glows in the dark. Is this a fact or an opinion? It's a fact. We can check it in a dark room. You will be happy all day long. Is this a fact or an opinion? It's an opinion. Pip says, Ads want to sell things, so they use big, happy words. Look for the facts first. Then decide for yourself. Now, phonics. Audible. Responsible. Aud and respons are not words by themselves, so they take ible. Debatable. Debate, drop the e, plus able. Questionable. Question plus able. Is it the best toy ever? That's debatable! Pip says, Ads want to sell things, so they use big, happy words. Look for the facts first. Then decide for yourself. Now, phonics. Audible. Responsible. Aud and respons are not words by themselves, so they usually take ible. Debatable. Debate, drop the e, plus able. Questionable. Question plus able. Is it the best toy ever? That's debatable! Be smart shoppers, everyone! | objective | The -ible rule is a tendency, not a law (flexible, sensible, capable); src/lib/curriculum/w29Phonics.ts already says ことが おおい |

| 866 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, That's a fact. We checked it in a book. Five lenses are shining, and Captain Pell has an idea. Should we have homework on weekends? Say it with me. Should we have homework on weekends? Nice! Riko says, Let's hear from everyone. Haru raises his hand. I think we should, because practice makes us better. Kiko says, I see your point, but we need time to rest. Birimo says, I partly agree with both of you! Pip writes everything down. Let's read two posts from The Lighthouse News. "Readers' Voices. Gus writes: I think we should have a little homework on weekends. One reason is that we remember new things better. For example, I practice spelling for ten minutes every Saturday. Now I make fewer mistakes. Gale writes: I see my brother's point, but I don't think we need weekend homework. Weekends are for rest, family, and playing outside. Our brains need a break, too. Then we can learn more on Monday!" Who wants homework on weekends? Gus. Gus practices spelling for ten minutes every Saturday. Is this a fact or an opinion? It's a fact. Our brains need a break. Is this a fact or an opinion? It's an opinion. Beautiful! Kiko asks, How should I end it? Riko says, Say your opinion again. It makes the paragraph strong. Riko adds more details, and her paragraph grows to about sixty words. Let's read it. "My Opinion, by Riko. I think we should read every day. First, it's fun. Books take us to new places, like dragon islands and snowy mountains. Second, we learn new words. For example, I learned the word spectacular in a story about the sea. Even ten minutes a day can help. So, let's read together! Reading every day makes our minds strong and happy." What are Riko's two reasons? Reading is fun, and we learn new words. What is her example? She learned the word spectacular. Riko learned the word spectacular in a story. Is this a fact or an opinion? | 872 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, Cars fly and robots work here. Today, we walk down Main Street with Nova. The street is very clean. There is no trash anywhere. Why? Look! A small green robot is rolling along the sidewalk. It has two soft brushes and a long arm. Nova says, This is a robot that cleans the streets. A robot that cleans（そうじする ロボット）. Say it with me. This is a robot that cleans the streets. Very good! The robot picks up a can. Then it picks up a paper cup. It picks up trash and sorts it. Cans go in one box, and paper goes in another box. Riko asks, What does it do at night? Nova says, It cleans the streets at night, too. It never sleeps! Let's read the sign on the robot. "Hello! I am Street Helper Number Seven. I clean Main Street every day and every night. I pick up cans, bottles, and paper. I sort them, and then I take them to the recycling center. Please put your trash in the trash cans. That makes my job easier. If you see me, please step aside. I move slowly, so I am safe for people and pets. Thank you for keeping our city clean!" Where does the robot take the trash? To the recycling center. Is the robot safe for pets? Yes, because it moves slowly. Is this a fact or an opinion? It's a fact. Now, phonics. Auto means self. Autobiography. A book about your own life. You write it yourself. Automate. To make a machine do a job by itself. Automation. Machines doing jobs by themselves. Autocorrect. It fixes your spelling by itself. | 875 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, That building makes its own energy. Today, we have an important job. A group of little ducklings is visiting the city with their teacher, Mrs. Waddle. Nova asks, Can you be their tour guides? Of course! Riko holds up a yellow flag. She says, Please follow me. We walk past Street Helper Number Seven and the Sun Tower. Then we arrive at a green park. Here we are! Kiko says, This is a park that has solar trees. Say it with me. This is a park that has solar trees. Perfect! The solar trees are tall, and their leaves are solar panels. Haru asks, Do you have any questions? A duckling says, Yes! What do the solar trees do? Haru reads the sign. "Solar Tree Park. Our solar trees are not real trees, but they work hard! Their leaves are solar panels. They give shade on hot days, so you can rest under them. They also make electricity from sunlight. You can charge bikes and scooters at the bottom of each tree. At night, the trees use the energy to light the paths. There are also real trees in the park, with birds and flowers. Please enjoy both kinds of trees!" What can you charge at the bottom of each tree? Bikes and scooters. What do the trees do at night? They light the paths. The ducklings say, Thank you, guides! Then they ask for our autographs. Look at your Expo Pass. Beep! The first star badge is shining! Now, phonics. Let's review auto. Auto means self. Automatic. Autograph. Autopilot. Automation. Say auto clearly. Great job, tour guides! | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, That building makes its own energy. | 876 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, This is a park that has solar trees. Today, Nova takes us on a job tour. We visit a bright studio full of colorful screens. A fox with round glasses waves at us. Her name is Ms. Lin. Nova says, She's a person who designs games. Say it with me. She's a person who designs games. Fantastic! Birimo is so excited. He loves games! Ms. Lin says, My job is to make games fun and fair. So Nova tells us, Her job is to make games fun and fair. Ms. Lin shows us her new game. Players help a little robot build a bridge. Kiko asks, How do you make a game? Ms. Lin shows us her planning board. Let's read it. "How I Design a Game. Step one: I think of an idea. My new game is about a robot who builds bridges. Step two: I draw the characters and the world. Step three: I test the game with children, and I listen to their opinions. Step four: I make it better. A good game is fun, but it is also fair. Everyone can win if they practice. My games also say, Time for a break, after thirty minutes. Our eyes and bodies need rest." What does Ms. Lin do in step three? She tests the game with children. What do her games say after thirty minutes? Time for a break! Riko says, I think that's a great idea because breaks are important. Now, phonics. Today's word part is trans. Trans means across or change. Transport. To carry things from one place to another. Transform. To change into something new. Translate. To change words into another language. Transfer. | 877 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, She's a person who designs games. Today, our job tour continues. Nova takes us to the City Helper Center. There are many different jobs here. In the first room, we meet a doctor and a nurse. Nova says, Doctors are people who help sick people. Say it with me. Doctors are people who help sick people. Very nice! In the next room, there is a big red fire truck. Haru says, Firefighters are people who put out fires. In the last room, a woman is fixing a robot arm. She is a robot repairer. Kiko asks, Who helps us when we're sick? Riko answers, Doctors and nurses do. On the wall, there is a big poster. Let's read it. "Thank You, City Helpers! Every day, many people help our city. Doctors and nurses help sick people get better. Firefighters put out fires and rescue people. Recycling workers collect cans and bottles, so they can be used again. Robot repairers fix our street robots and buses. Robots can do many jobs, but they can't do everything. People are still very important! Next time you see a city helper, please say thank you." Who collects cans and bottles? Recycling workers. Who fixes the street robots? Robot repairers. Birimo says, I think firefighters are the bravest people because they help people in danger. Is that a fact or an opinion? It's an opinion. Now, phonics. Trans means across or change. Transmit. To send a message or a signal across. Transplant. To move a plant to a new place. Transportation. Buses, trains, and cars. They carry people from place to place. Let's say thank you to our city helpers! | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, She's a person who designs games. Today, our job tour continues. Nova takes us to the City Helper Center. There are many different jobs here. In the first room, we meet a doctor and a nurse. Nova says, Doctors are people who help sick people. Say it with me. Doctors are people who help sick people. Very nice! In the next room, there is a big red fire truck. Haru says, Firefighters are people who put out fires. In the last room, a woman is fixing a robot arm. She is a robot mechanic. Kiko asks, Who helps us when we're sick? Riko answers, Doctors and nurses do. On the wall, there is a big poster. Let's read it. "Thank You, City Helpers! Every day, many people help our city. Doctors and nurses help sick people get better. Firefighters put out fires and rescue people. Recycling workers collect cans and bottles, so we can use them again. Robot mechanics fix our street robots and buses. Robots can do many jobs, but they can't do everything. People are still very important! Next time you see a city helper, please say thank you." Who collects cans and bottles? Recycling workers. Who fixes the street robots? Robot mechanics. Birimo says, I think firefighters are the bravest people because they help people in danger. Is that a fact or an opinion? It's an opinion. Now, phonics. Trans means across or change. Transmit. To send a message or a signal across. Transplant. To move a plant to a new place. Transportation. Buses, trains, and cars. They carry people from place to place. Let's say thank you to our city helpers! | A+C | 'repairer' is rare in US English and awkward to say; 'they can be used' read as if the workers are used |

| 879 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, This is the girl who invented the robot dog. Today, Nova has a quiz for us. It's called Who Am I? Nova reads a hint, and we guess the job. Nova says, I'm a person who works at night and keeps you safe. Say it with me. I'm a person who works at night and keeps you safe. Excellent! Haru raises his hand. Are you a police officer? Nova says, That's right! It can be a night guard, too. Now it's Kiko's turn. She says, Give me one more hint, please. Let's read the quiz cards. "Quiz Card One: I'm a person who gets up very early. I make bread and cakes, and my shop smells wonderful. Quiz Card Two: I'm a person who grows rice and vegetables. I work outside in the sun and the rain. Quiz Card Three: I'm a person who works in space. I float in my ship, and I can see the whole Earth from my window. Quiz Card Four: I'm a robot who helps visitors. I can float, and my eyes shine blue." Who is Card One? A baker. Card Two? A farmer. Card Three? An astronaut. And Card Four? It's Nova! Everyone laughs. Riko says, I think astronaut is the most exciting job because you can see the Earth from space. Is that a fact or an opinion? It's an opinion. Now, phonics. Trans means across or change. Translator. A person who changes words into another language. Transformer. A machine that changes electricity. Transmission. Sending a message or a signal across the air. Now you can make a Who Am I quiz, too! | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, This is the girl who invented the robot dog. Today, Nova has a quiz for us. It's called Who Am I? Nova reads a hint, and we guess the job. Nova says, I'm a person who works at night and keeps you safe. Say it with me. I'm a person who works at night and keeps you safe. Excellent! Haru raises his hand. Are you a police officer? Nova says, That's right! It can be a night guard, too. Now it's Kiko's turn. She says, Give me one more hint, please. Let's read the quiz cards. "Quiz Card One: I'm a person who gets up very early. I make bread and cakes, and my shop smells wonderful. Quiz Card Two: I'm a person who grows rice and vegetables. I work outside in the sun and the rain. Quiz Card Three: I'm a person who works in space. I float in my ship, and I can see the whole Earth from my window. Quiz Card Four: I'm a robot that helps visitors. I can float, and my eyes shine blue." Who is Card One? A baker. Card Two? A farmer. Card Three? An astronaut. And Card Four? It's Nova! Everyone laughs. Riko says, I think an astronaut has the most exciting job because you can see the Earth from space. Is that a fact or an opinion? It's an opinion. Now, phonics. Trans means across or change. Translator. A person who changes words into another language. Transformer. A machine that changes electricity. Transmission. Sending a message or a signal through the air. Now you can make a Who Am I quiz, too! | objective | robot takes 'that' (L876 rule: things get that or which); missing article 'an astronaut'; 'through the air' (JA くうきの なかを とおして) |

| 882 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, The problem is that older people can't carry heavy bags. Today, we go to Tess's lab. It is full of tools, wires, and old robot parts. Sprocket runs around and barks happily. Tess puts a big board on the wall. She says, This is our idea board. Every idea is welcome. Ms. Emma asks, Do you have any ideas? Haru says, How about a robot arm? Kiko says, How about a moving sidewalk on Hill Street? Birimo says, How about balloons? Then Riko stands up. She says, My idea is a bag that follows you. Say it with me. My idea is a bag that follows you. Wonderful! Tess claps her hands and says, That's a great idea! Let's draw it. Let's read the idea board. "Tess's Idea Board. Idea 1, from Haru: a robot arm that lifts heavy bags. It is strong, but it is very big. Idea 2, from Kiko: a moving sidewalk on Hill Street. It helps everyone, but it takes years to build. Idea 3, from Birimo: balloons that lift bags into the sky. It is fun, but windy days are dangerous. Idea 4, from Riko: a bag that follows you. It is small, and it can go anywhere." Whose idea is the moving sidewalk? It's Kiko's idea. Which idea can go anywhere? Riko's bag. The friends talk about every idea and give reasons. In the end, they choose Riko's idea. Small ideas can help a lot. Now, phonics. Remember, with words that end in ic, say the part just before ic strongly. Magnetic. NET is strong. Electronic. TRON is strong. Energetic. GET is strong. Futuristic. IS is strong. The City of Tomorrow looks so futuristic! Next time, let's find out how the bag works. | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, The problem is that older people can't carry heavy bags. Today, we go to Tess's lab. It is full of tools, wires, and old robot parts. Sprocket runs around and barks happily. Kiko says, How about a moving sidewalk on Hill Street? Birimo says, How about balloons? Then Riko stands up. She says, My idea is a bag that follows you. Say it with me. Idea 3, from Birimo: balloons that lift bags into the sky. It is fun, but windy days are dangerous. Idea 4, from Riko: a bag that follows you. It is small, and it can go anywhere." Whose idea is the moving sidewalk? It's Kiko's idea. Which idea can go anywhere? Riko's bag. The friends talk about every idea and give reasons. In the end, they choose Riko's idea. Next time, let's find out how the bag works. | A+C (block rule) | Ms. Emma reads her own script, so "Ms. Emma says / asks / explains / has" -> "I say / I ask / I explain / I have" (same as W17-28); JA エマ先生「 -> わたし「 |

Oh no! Haru asks, What went wrong? Riko says, The camera saw the cat. Kiko says, And the wheels are too small for the stairs. Tess smiles. She says, Mistakes help me learn. It didn't work well, so we changed the design. Say it with me. It didn't work well, so we changed the design. Excellent! We turn off the camera and put on bigger wheels. Then we say, Let's try again. This time, it worked! Let's read Tess's test report. "Test Report: The Follow-Me Bag. Test 1, Monday. The bag followed Mr. Shell for ten meters. Then it saw a cat and followed the cat into a garden. Then Haru says, One good point is that robots never get tired. One problem is that they can break. Say it with me. One good point is that robots never get tired. One problem is that they can break. Very good! Nova nods. That's true. I need energy every day, and sometimes I need repairs. And I can't give you a warm hug like a friend. Ms. Emma asks, What do you think about robots? Let's read the big sign at the Robot Station. "Robots in the City of Tomorrow. There are about two thousand robots in our city. They clean streets, drive buses, and check bridges at night. Robots never get tired, and they can do jobs that are dangerous for people. But robots can break, and they need a lot of energy. They also cannot understand feelings the way friends do. That is why robots and people work together here. Robots do the heavy jobs, and people make the big decisions." How many robots are there in the city? About two thousand. Who makes the big decisions? | 887 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, One good point is that robots never get tired. One problem is that they can break. Today, we walk through the Sky Market. There are screens everywhere. There are screens on the walls, on the buses, and even on the tables! Birimo watches funny videos for a long time. Then he rubs his eyes. Kiko says, My eyes feel tired, too. Nova floats over. Beep! Screens are useful, but we need breaks. Say it with me. Screens are useful, but we need breaks. Nice! Haru asks, How much screen time is OK? Nova says, It's different for every family. Talk with your family and make a plan together. Then Ms. Emma says, Let's look far away and rest our eyes. We look at the clouds and the mountains far away. Ah, that feels much better. Let's read a poster from the City Health Center. "Screen Smart Tips. One: Every twenty minutes, look at something far away for twenty seconds. Two: Sit up straight, and keep the screen at arm's length. Three: Turn off screens one hour before bed. Your brain needs quiet time to sleep well. Four: Play outside every day. Sunlight and running are good for your body and your eyes. Screens are useful, but your eyes need breaks!" How often should you look far away? Every twenty minutes. When should you turn off screens? One hour before bed. Now, phonics. Some short words are units. We don't read the letters. We read the whole word. km. We say kilometers. cm. We say centimeters. kg. We say kilograms. mm. We say millimeters. The Sky Market is two kilometers long! Next time, we learn how to be safe online. | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, One good point is that robots never get tired. One problem is that they can break. | 891 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, I think technology is helpful because it saves time. However, we shouldn't use it too much. Today, Nova takes us to a new place. It's Tomorrow Hall, a museum of predictions. A prediction is a guess about the future. On the walls, there are big screens with pictures of the year 2050. Nova floats to the first screen and says, Beep! In 2050, people will probably live longer. Say it with me. In 2050, people will probably live longer. Excellent! Riko asks, Why do you think so? Nova answers, Because medicine will be better, and people will eat healthier food. Kiko asks, What will life be like in 2050? Haru says, I think we'll travel to the moon for vacation. Birimo says, I think robots will probably cook all my snacks! Let's read the card under the screen. "Prediction Number One. In 2050, people will probably live longer than today. Why? Doctors are learning more every year. New medicine can stop many sicknesses early. Many people also exercise more and eat more vegetables. But nobody can see the future for sure. A prediction is not a fact. It is a smart guess with good reasons. What do you think? Write your own prediction on a paper star and stick it on the wall!" Is a prediction a fact? No, it's a smart guess. What do people eat more of? Vegetables. Doctors are learning more every year. Is this a fact or an opinion? It's a fact. Now, phonics. Today, we listen to fast, friendly English. I'm gonna try it. Gonna means going to. We're gonna be late. I wanna see the robot. Wanna means want to. Do you wanna play? Friends talk like this, but when we write, we use going to and want to. Let's make a prediction star together! | Hello, everyone! In our future city, there will be many parks and quiet buses. Wonderful! Haru gives a reason. One reason is that clean air is good for everyone. Riko adds, There will be a library which floats in the sky. Birimo says, There will be a giant cake shop, of course! Nova asks, What will there be for older people? Kiko answers, There will be benches and gentle hills. Let's read their plan. "Our Future City Plan. In our future city, there will be many parks and quiet buses. The buses will run on clean energy. Every home will have a roof garden, and the streets will have solar trees for shade. There will be a sky library, a big sports field, and benches for older people. One problem is that it will cost a lot of money. So we will build the city little by little, together." What will the buses run on? Clean energy. What is one problem? It will cost a lot of money. Nova beeps happily. The fifth star badge on our Expo Passes is shining! Now, phonics. Let's review. I'm gonna try it. Gonna means going to. USB. We say U, S, B, one letter at a time. Futuristic. | 896 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, In our future city, there will be many parks and quiet buses. Today, we visit Tess and Sprocket again. Tess is fixing a robot arm in her lab. She talks very fast. Tess says, I'm gonna try it! Haru looks confused. Gonna? What does that mean? Nova says, Beep! Gonna is a short way to say going to. Say it with me. "I'm gonna try it!" means "I'm going to try it." Great job! Then Tess asks, Do you wanna play a robot game? Riko answers, Sure! I'm gonna win! Let's read the note on the lab wall. "Fast Friend Talk, by Tess. When I talk with my friends, I often use short words. Gonna means going to. Wanna means want to. Gotta means got to. Kinda means kind of. We say these words with friends, but we write going to and want to. In a letter, a report, or a test, please use the full words. Then people all around the world can understand you. Sprocket only says woof, so he doesn't need this note!" What does wanna mean? Want to. When do we use the full words? In a letter, a report, or a test. Gonna means going to. Is this a fact or an opinion? It's a fact. Birimo says, I'm gonna eat lunch now! Ha ha. Remember, gonna is fine with friends, but not in your notebook. Now, phonics. We're gonna be late. Do you wanna play? TV. We say T, V. Electric. Say e, LEC, tric. Transform. Trans means change, so transform means change shape. Autograph. Auto means self, so it's your own name. | 897 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, "I'm gonna try it!" means "I'm going to try it." Today, Nova gives us the city newspaper, The Tomorrow Times. Let's read the top article together. "Tree Robot Helps Bald Mountain. Last spring, a small robot named Rooty went to work on Bald Mountain. Strong winds knocked down the trees there five years ago. Rooty can dig a hole, plant a seed, and give it water. It plants three hundred trees a day. It uses a camera to find good soil. Some people worry that robots will take people's jobs. But here, local children plant trees with Rooty every weekend. Now the mountain is turning green again." Kiko says, This article is about a robot that plants trees. Say it with me. This article is about a robot that plants trees. Excellent! Riko asks, What's the main idea? Haru answers, The main idea is that robots can help nature. Where did the robot plant the trees? It planted them on a mountain. How many trees does Rooty plant a day? Three hundred. Some people worry about jobs. Is this a fact or an opinion? It's a fact that some people feel this way. Birimo says, I think Rooty is the best robot ever! That's an opinion. Good readers always find the main idea first. Then they carefully look for details, like numbers, names, and places. Now, phonics. You gotta see this. Gotta means got to. GPS. We say G, P, S. It helps us find places. Scientific. Say sci, en, TIF, ic. Transmission. Trans means across, so it sends something across.
